RBM.Gauss.witEdge, RBM.Gauss.witTensor, RBM.Gauss.sum_witEdge, RBM.Gauss.norm_witEdge_le, RBM.Gauss.witEdge_eq_zero, RBM.Gauss.norm_witTensor_le, RBM.Gauss.witTensor_zero_arg, RBM.Gauss.witTensor_ne_zero, RBM.Gauss.sumZero_witTensor, RBM.Gauss.Qop_witTensor, RBM.Gauss.fastDecay_witTensor, RBM.Gauss.gridS_Q716_witness, RBM.Gauss.momNorm_Uker_Qop_le, RBM.Gauss.momNorm_Uker_commS_le, RBM.Gauss.momNorm_Uker_PsumVarthetaDot_le, RBM.Gauss.momNorm_Uker_QQ_le}
  \leanok\uses{def:5.12, lem:7.3, thm:step3, thm:momentduhamel, lem:momentduhamelrhs}
  Under the integrated hierarchy (5.20)/(5.91) with the quadratic-variation/BDG bounds
  (5.85)(5.103) as the only random inputs, and with (5.77) and (5.75) as hypotheses:
  (5.92) holds for every $n\ge2$, i.e.\ \texttt{Step3.Lemma514}, so Steps 3--5 no longer assume it.

  \emph{The same conclusion on the moment route, with no \texttt{SumZeroDyn.Hierarchy} and no
  \texttt{Lemma510} field anywhere in the file, and using only $0\le s_N$.}
  \texttt{lemma514\_of\_momentDuhamel} runs from a \texttt{MomentDuhamel.Hyp}, and
  \texttt{flow\_sharpLmK\_of\_momentDuhamel} feeds the result into Steps 4--5 as a bare
  application.  Two methodological points: \texttt{unifDomIcc\_of\_forall\_stochDom} exchanges
  ``$\prec$ along every endpoint sequence'' for \texttt{UnifDomIcc}, which is legitimate only
  because $\tau,D$ precede the eventual quantifier and no $\exists C$ intervenes -- and the
  converse is proved too, so nothing is strengthened in passing; and
  \texttt{stochDom\_xiLK\_of\_nonneg} weakens $0<s_N$ to $0\le s_N$, which is where this route
  avoids reopening the $u=0$ seam.  \emph{The exchange does not apply on the right-hand side}:
  \texttt{hrhs} concludes $\exists C,\ \forall^\infty N$, with $C$ outside, so the choice argument
  fails there; \texttt{rhs514At\_forall\_of\_moment\_inputs} shows it is not needed.

  \emph{The H\"older input is a theorem, but with one quantifier correction.} The deterministic
  modulus \texttt{hHol\_flow} -- $u\mapsto(W\ell_u\eta_u)^m\|(L-K)_{u,\sigma,a}\|$ is
  $\gamma=\tfrac12$-H\"older on a high-probability event -- rests on the stretching modulus of a
  product of $m$ resolvents (already in the library), on \texttt{norm\_Kgen\_sub\_le}, where
  (2.48) makes $K$ \emph{Lipschitz} rather than $\tfrac12$-H\"older, and on
  \texttt{abs\_ellHat\_sub\_le}, where $\hat\ell=\min((1-u)^{-1/2},L)$ is only
  $\tfrac12$-H\"older as $u\to1$ -- the same exponent the $\sqrt u$ of the flow gives.  But
  \texttt{lemma514\_forall\_of\_momentDuhamel} orders $(K,\gamma)$ \emph{before} $\forall m$, i.e.
  one constant for all loop lengths, and that is unobtainable: the modulus of $m$ resolvents
  carries $\eta^{-(m+1)}$ and $\eta^{-1}$ is a positive power of $N$, so the constant grows
  geometrically in $m$.  Its proof only ever uses $\texttt{hHol}(n+2)$, so the correct interface
  is the single-$m$ \texttt{lemma514\_of\_momentDuhamel};
  \texttt{lemma514\_forall\_of\_hHol\_flow} assembles the same conclusion by giving each $m$ its
  own constant.

  \emph{Still hypothetical.}  A \texttt{MomentDuhamel.Hyp} is not yet produced in full
  (\ref{thm:momentduhamel}).  Among the right-hand side inputs, \texttt{hkerlt}/\texttt{hker2lt}
  are unconditional, but \texttt{hkerC}/\texttt{hker2C} hold \emph{if and only if} the window is
  short, $t_N-s_N\le\kappa(1-t_N)$, with $C_k=1+\kappa$ -- and that condition is not merely
  underived, it is \emph{false} where it would be used; see the next paragraph.  The polynomial
  envelope for $K$ is instantiated only at loop length $2$; lengths $\ge3$ are covered by (2.59)
  but with a $0<t$ restriction that is not yet bridged.  Finally, \texttt{Lemma514Premises} must
  stay: quantifying instead over all $(\Lambda,\Phi)$ collapses the control at $\Lambda=\Phi=0$
  and makes the theorem vacuous.

  \emph{The short-window condition is false on the paper's own grid, and the reason is that the
  moment route is using the wrong lemma.}  On the grid $1-s_k=W^{-k\tau'}$ of p.\,24 one
  has $(t-s)/(1-t)=W^{\tau'}-1$ \emph{exactly}, so an $N$-independent $C_k$ would force
  $W_N^{\tau'}\le C_k$ against $W_N\to\infty$.  Hence \texttt{hkerC\_flow} and
  \texttt{hker2C\_flow} are true theorems whose window slot is unsatisfiable on the assembly
  window, and filling the right-hand side with them would make Step 3 vacuous.  The root cause is
  structural: when $\|\xi\|=1$ the left side of \texttt{hkerC} is identically $\eta_u/\eta_w$, so
  the constant it demands is $(\eta_s/\eta_t)^{n+2}$ -- which is exactly Lemma 7.1's prefactor.
  And (5.92)'s own remark that there is \emph{no} prefactor depending on $\eta_s/\eta_t$ is
  precisely the statement that Lemma 5.14 may not use Lemma 7.1: reading \S5.5, every term of its
  proof projects through $Q_u$ and then uses (7.16), never (7.1).  Writing $\rho=rR$, the three
  tiers already present in the library pay $\rho^n=R^nr^n$ for (7.1), $R\,r^n$ for (7.14) and
  $r^n$ for (7.16), and it is the $r^n$ that cancels the $A^{-n}$ normalization -- that
  cancellation \emph{is} the absence of a prefactor.  The moment route is using the weakest of the
  three and overpays $R^{n+2}$.

  Absorbing the loss into a constant $C\,R^k$ does not close either.  There is an explicit triple
  satisfying all seven fields of \texttt{Step3.Scales} for which $R^nA_s^{1/2}>A$ at every
  $n\ge5$, so the extra $R^n$ is already outside the budget; and, more decisively, that power of
  $R$ multiplies $\Lambda^{1/2}$ while the floor in the Step 3 induction is independent of the
  second index, so raising that index -- the channel Step 3's double induction uses to absorb
  powers of $R$ -- is closed against this particular loss.  Retreating to (7.14) brings the loss
  down to $R^1$, which does fit inside \texttt{Scales}, but it multiplies $\Lambda^{1/2}$ just the
  same and still breaks the floor.  Only (7.16)'s $r^n$ is lossless.  \emph{The paper is
  self-consistent here; the gap is on the formalization's side} (paper-deltas \#131).  What has to
  be built is the moment form of (7.16), on top of
  \texttt{norm\_Uker\_fastDecay\_le\_sumZero} and \texttt{norm\_Uker\_sumZero\_scale\_le}, plus a
  consumer for \texttt{Hyp.momentDuhamelQ}, which to date has none; two seams are known in
  advance, that sum-zero after $Q$ is a free algebraic identity while fast decay holds only on a
  high-probability event, and that the left side of \texttt{momentDuhamelQ} is $Q_v\circ(L-K)_v$,
  so returning to $(L-K)_v$ still needs the projection's other half.  \emph{Honestly}: this
  paragraph is a read-only audit whose seven theorems -- the grid computation, the contradiction,
  the identity for the left side and the \texttt{Scales} witness -- were compiled but
  \emph{not} committed to the library, so no Lean name is claimed for them here.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $Q_t\circ(L-K)$ satisfies (5.88); the $\dot\vartheta$ term is controlled by Ward's identity
  (Lemma 3.6, proved for $L-K$), the commutator by (5.99), the evolution by (7.16) Case 2 on sum-zero
  tensors; non-alternating $\sigma$ use (7.16) Case 1; the martingale by BDG.
  On the moment route the martingale is absent; (5.92)'s $w$-dependence is monotone in opposite
  directions on the two sides, so \texttt{hnum\_of\_endpoints} reduces the whole window to the two
  endpoints $w=t_N$ and $w=s_N$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Theorem 2.21 in the gained form the six steps actually produce]\label{thm:thm221-gain}
  \lean{RBM.Cond272', RBM.Thm221', RBM.Cond272'.toCond272, RBM.Thm221.toThm221', RBM.Band.eventually_flow_grid', RBM.gridT_step_2_72_gain, RBM.flow_grid_2_72_gain, RBM.Bounds_of_Thm221', RBM.stochDom_norm_Lval_of_Thm221', RBM.SpecSeq.bounds', RBM.localSemicircleLaw_of_Thm221', RBM.quantumDiffusion_of_Thm221', RBM.QDExpect.of_Thm221', RBM.theorem2_5_of_Thm221'}
  \leanok\uses{thm:2.3-2.4, thm:2.5-2.6, def:flow-hypotheses, lem:flow-scales}
  Theorem 2.21 as stated takes the bare (2.72) as its regularity condition, but every consumer
  inside the six steps needs the version carrying an $N^c$ gain, which is strictly stronger.  So
  the statement as written is not provable from what the steps deliver, and the point of
  \texttt{Cond272'} -- which is the steps' own condition word for word -- and \texttt{Thm221'} is
  to make it provable at all.  The unprimed \texttt{Cond272} and \texttt{Thm221} are untouched,
  and the direction is recorded both ways: \texttt{Cond272'.toCond272} and
  \texttt{Thm221.toThm221'} say the gained condition is stronger, hence \texttt{Thm221'} is the
  \emph{weaker} theorem and nothing downstream is lost.  The downstream restatements --
  Lemmas 2.18--2.20 and (2.61) (\texttt{Bounds\_of\_Thm221'},
  \texttt{stochDom\_norm\_Lval\_of\_Thm221'}), then Theorems 2.3/2.4 and 2.5
  (\texttt{SpecSeq.bounds'}, \texttt{localSemicircleLaw\_of\_Thm221'},
  \texttt{quantumDiffusion\_of\_Thm221'}, \texttt{theorem2\_5\_of\_Thm221'}) -- have conclusions
  word for word unchanged, checked by \texttt{rfl} probes.  Theorem 2.21 enters the whole assembly
  at exactly one place, the spectral-sequence bounds.

  The gain itself comes from the time grid of p.\,24: \texttt{gridT\_step\_2\_72\_gain} shows the
  grid step carries an arbitrary gain, \texttt{flow\_grid\_2\_72\_gain} and
  \texttt{eventually\_flow\_grid'} produce it along the flow, and the concrete value
  $c=\min(\tau,1)/32$ comes straight from (2.2)'s $W\ge N^{1/2+c_B}$, with no detour through the
  dimension.  \ \emph{One constraint that dictates the shape of the statement}: the grid's gain is
  bounded above by roughly $\tau/16$, so $c$ cannot be asked for arbitrarily large; \texttt{Thm221'}
  therefore has to hold for every arbitrarily small $c>0$, which is why $c$ is quantified
  \emph{inside} \texttt{step} rather than outside the structure.  This is a deviation of the
  formalization from the paper's wording only (paper-deltas \#125); the literal, un-gained route
  is a separate open item, and this node describes the tree as it stands.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  The proof of the grid estimate already contained a gain internally, $60\tau'<\tau$ making it a
  positive power of $W$; exposing it needed no new mathematics, only a primed field and the
  observation that $W\ge\sqrt{N/2}$ converts it into a power of $N$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Theorem 2.21 without (2.71): the p.\,25 remark, Steps 1--5 only]\label{thm:2.21-no-el}
  \lean{RBM.BoundsCore_zero, RBM.BoundsCore.congr, RBM.Thm221NoEL, RBM.Thm221NoEL.step, RBM.Thm221NoEL', RBM.Thm221NoEL'.step, RBM.Thm221NoEL.toThm221NoEL', RBM.Thm221NoEL.step_boundsCore, RBM.BoundsCore_of_Thm221NoEL', RBM.BoundsCore_of_Thm221NoEL, RBM.stochDom_norm_Lval_of_Thm221NoEL', RBM.Band.scale_zero_ge_rpow, RBM.Band.cond272Reg_grid, RBM.Band.eventually_rpow_le_scale, RBM.boundsCore_hyp_consistent, RBM.boundsCore_gauss_witness, RBM.BoundsCore_of_flow, RBM.boundsCore_step_of_flow, RBM.boundsCore_step_of_inputs, RBM.thm221NoEL'_of_inputs, RBM.SpecSeq.boundsCore, RBM.localLaw_of_boundsCore, RBM.loop1_of_boundsCore, RBM.partialTrace_of_boundsCore, RBM.loop2_of_boundsCore, RBM.quantumDiffusion_pm_of_boundsCore, RBM.quantumDiffusion_pp_of_boundsCore, RBM.trace_of_boundsCore, RBM.localSemicircleLaw_of_Thm221NoEL', RBM.quantumDiffusion_pm_pp_of_Thm221NoEL', RBM.StepGlue.eventually_R4_le_scale_of_cond272, RBM.StepGlue.aprioriDecay_of_jS_of_cond272, RBM.StepGlue.localLaw_of_scale_facts, RBM.Step2PP.harith_flowAs_of_reg, RBM.Step2PP.flow_sharpLoop_glue_of_cond272', RBM.Step2PP.flow_steps45_glue_of_cond272', RBM.Step2PP.flow_sharpLoop_glue_flowAs_of_cond272', RBM.Step2PP.flow_steps45_glue_flowAs_of_reg', RBM.MomentDuhamelCut.aprioriDecay_cut_of_cond272, RBM.MomentDuhamelCut.step2_cut_of_reg, RBM.boundsCore_step_of_flow_reg, RBM.boundsCore_step_of_inputs_reg, RBM.thm221NoEL_of_inputs, RBM.Thm221NoEL.step_boundsCore_reg, RBM.cond272Reg_grid_step_domain}
  \leanok\uses{thm:thm221-gain, thm:2.18-from-2.21, thm:2.3-2.4, def:flow-hypotheses, def:gauss-model}
  The paper remarks on p.\,25, just after Step 6, that (2.71) is not needed for Steps 1--5, so
  Theorem 2.21 holds with (2.71) removed from both the assumption and the statement.  In Lean this
  is \texttt{Thm221NoEL} over the existing \texttt{BoundsCore} -- the three-field package already in
  \texttt{Flow/Hypotheses.lean}, which is exactly \texttt{Bounds} with the \texttt{expect} field
  dropped; no primed copy of the package was needed.  The remark is a claim about the \emph{proof},
  so \texttt{Thm221NoEL} is an independent structure and \emph{not} comparable with
  \texttt{Thm221}: the latter's step demands (2.71) at time $s$, which \texttt{BoundsCore} does not
  carry (paper-deltas \#T204a).  Lemmas 2.18--2.20 and (2.61) are re-run over \texttt{BoundsCore}
  with (2.62) dropped, and Theorems 2.3 and 2.4's (2.6)(2.7) are restated on it, their conclusions
  word for word unchanged, checked by \texttt{rfl} probes against the \texttt{Bounds} versions.
  Theorem 2.4's (2.8)(2.9) and Theorem 2.5 still need (2.71) and are deliberately left on
  \texttt{Bounds} rather than hung on an empty hypothesis (paper-deltas \#T204b).

  \emph{What the assembly can actually produce}: \texttt{Thm221NoEL'}, whose regularity condition is
  the steps' own \texttt{Cond272'}.  \texttt{Thm221NoEL}, which takes D13's \texttt{Cond272Reg}, is
  not yet produced: Step 1 already matches it word for word, but Steps 2--5 consume
  \texttt{Cond272'} literally and the bridge gives no gain at all at $(a,b)=(1,30)$.  The
  satisfiability witness is explicit and non-degenerate: \texttt{boundsCore\_gauss\_witness} builds
  \texttt{BoundsCore} for the Gaussian model at the growing dimensions
  ($L\asymp N^{1/4}$, $W\asymp N^{3/4}$) and proves the right-hand side really is $\prec N^{-n/2}$,
  not $\prec1$; \texttt{cond272Reg\_grid} shows the paper's own time grid satisfies
  \texttt{Cond272Reg} at every step inside D13's window, the joint constraint on which T195's
  short-window condition failed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Nothing in Steps 1--5 reads the \texttt{expect} field, so the existing producers compose over the
  smaller package unchanged; the iteration's proof script is copied verbatim, and the downstream
  restatements are the same terms with a weaker hypothesis, which is what the \texttt{rfl} probes
  check.
\end{proof}

  \leanok\uses{lem:zero-mode, def:flow-hypotheses, thm:2.5-2.6}
  $\tilde S=(1-\zeta_U)S+\zeta_U/N$ (7.25); the scale arithmetic (7.30)--(7.32); the continuity
  bootstrap (7.33)--(7.36); (7.39)--(7.44) re-accounted; (7.45)(7.46) give (7.27)(7.28); and (7.47),

\begin{proof}\leanok
  One continuity argument over all lengths $2\le n\le n_0$ (first failure time); the GUE-phase 2-loop
  $K$ in closed form, matching (2.57) at $t_1$ and solving (7.33); the error scale converted with
  $\eta_{t_0}=t_0^{1/2}\eta$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Moments $\Rightarrow\prec$, and the $N^{-C}$ time net (moment route)]\label{lem:moment-dom}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.meas_gt_le_of_moment, RBM.Gauss.MomentDom, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_of_momentDom, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_one_of_momentDom, RBM.Gauss.netSize, RBM.Gauss.netPt, RBM.Gauss.exists_netPt_close, RBM.Gauss.card_net_le, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_Icc_of_holder, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_Icc_of_lipschitz}
  \leanok\uses{def:2.1-i}
  Markov: $\mathbb E|Y_N|^{2p}\le C_p N^{\varepsilon p}\Phi^{2p}$ for all $p$ gives $Y\prec\Phi$.
  On an interval, an $N^{-C}$ net plus a pointwise H\"older-$\gamma$ modulus in $u$ gives
  Definition 2.1(i) with the uncountable union over $u$ inside the probability.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Chebyshev at each net point and the finite union bound; between net points the deterministic
  modulus costs $N^{-C'}$. Net size $\lceil N^{(K+B+1)/\gamma}\rceil+1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The Gaussian band matrix and the flow $H_u=\sqrt u\,X$ (moment route)]\label{def:gauss-model}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.Dims, RBM.Gauss.idxKey, RBM.Gauss.idxKey_injective, RBM.Gauss.Coord, RBM.Gauss.gvar, RBM.Gauss.P, RBM.Gauss.isProbabilityMeasure_P, RBM.Gauss.P_map_eval, RBM.Gauss.P_map_restrict, RBM.Gauss.Xentry, RBM.Gauss.Xmat, RBM.Gauss.Xentry_swap, RBM.Gauss.Xmat_isHermitian, RBM.Gauss.measurable_Xentry, RBM.Gauss.integral_normSq_Xentry, RBM.Gauss.Hflow, RBM.Gauss.Hflow_zero, RBM.Gauss.Hflow_isHermitian, RBM.Gauss.band, RBM.Gauss.sample, RBM.Gauss.Hflow_sub, RBM.Gauss.norm_Hflow_sub_apply, RBM.Gauss.norm_Hflow_sub, RBM.Gauss.abs_sqrt_sub_sqrt_le, RBM.Gauss.OpNormBound, RBM.Gauss.rpow_div_le_of_pow_le, RBM.Gauss.Dims.bandwidth_three, RBM.Gauss.Dims.example, RBM.Gauss.Dims.nonempty, RBM.Gauss.Dims.growL, RBM.Gauss.Dims.growW, RBM.Gauss.Dims.three_le_growL, RBM.Gauss.Dims.growL_pow_le, RBM.Gauss.Dims.twentyseven_mul_growL_le, RBM.Gauss.Dims.growW_eq, RBM.Gauss.Dims.dim_grow, RBM.Gauss.Dims.pow_five_le_pow_eight, RBM.Gauss.Dims.bandwidth_grow, RBM.Gauss.Dims.exampleGrow, RBM.Gauss.Dims.tendsto_growL, RBM.Gauss.Dims.tendsto_growW, RBM.Gauss.Dims.band_example, RBM.Gauss.Dims.sample_example, RBM.Gauss.Dims.nonempty_Omega, RBM.Gauss.Dims.nonempty_Idx, RBM.Gauss.Dims.exists_Sblk_pos, RBM.Gauss.Dims.opNormBound_example}
  \leanok\uses{def:model, def:SB}
  $X$ Hermitian with independent entries above the diagonal and $\mathbb E|X_{ij}|^2=S_{ij}$
  (\texttt{integral\_normSq\_Xentry}); $H_u:=\sqrt u\,X$ realizes (2.34) at one time and gives a
  \texttt{Sample} whose three fields are theorems; the deterministic modulus
  $\|H_u-H_{u'}\|=|\sqrt u-\sqrt{u'}|\,\|X\|$ feeds the time net.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Generator identity for $H_u=\sqrt u\,X$ (moment route)]\label{thm:generator}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.MatrixStein, RBM.Gauss.TestFun, RBM.Gauss.usedCoord, RBM.Gauss.Bmat, RBM.Gauss.Xmat_eq_sum, RBM.Gauss.Xmat_update, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_coordD1_update, RBM.Gauss.coordD2, RBM.Gauss.wirtSecond, RBM.Gauss.sum_used_eq_sum_pairs, RBM.Gauss.sum_used_eq_sum_pairs_coordD2, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_integral_Phi, RBM.Gauss.deriv_integral_Phi, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_integral_Phi_pairs, RBM.Gauss.hermCLM, RBM.Gauss.isHermitian_hermCLM, RBM.Gauss.norm_green_le, RBM.Gauss.iteratedDeriv_lineInverse, RBM.Gauss.norm_iteratedDeriv_green_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_lineInversePow_le, RBM.Gauss.fderiv_comp_clm_apply, RBM.Gauss.fderiv2_comp_clm_apply, RBM.Gauss.norm_fderiv_comp_clm_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_fderiv2_comp_clm_le, RBM.Gauss.hermFun, RBM.Gauss.coordD2_hermFun, RBM.Gauss.wirtSecond_hermFun, RBM.Gauss.TestFun', RBM.Gauss.TestFun.toTestFun', RBM.Gauss.TestFun'.herm, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_integral_Phi', RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_integral_Phi_pairs', RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_integral_Phi_pairs_of_herm}
  \leanok\uses{def:gauss-model, lem:moment-dom}
  $\partial_u\mathbb E[\Phi(H_u)]=\tfrac12\sum_{ij}S_{ij}\mathbb E[\partial_{ij}\partial_{ji}\Phi(H_u)]$
  for $u>0$, from Stein's identity coordinatewise; plus the global resolvent bounds
  $\|G\|\le\eta^{-1}$ and $\|\partial^k G\|\le k!\,\eta^{-(k+1)}\|A\|^k$ that make every
  domination hypothesis a constant.

  \texttt{TestFun} quantifies its bounds over \emph{all} matrices, which the real integrands do
  not satisfy: $(M-z)^{-1}$ is unbounded off the Hermitian set, and at a singular non-Hermitian
  $M$ the integrand is not even continuous.  \texttt{TestFun'} asks for the bounds only at
  Hermitian $M$ and only \texttt{ContDiffAt} there, \texttt{TestFun.toTestFun'} keeps every
  existing supply, and the identity for the new class is a \emph{corollary} of the old one, not a
  second dominated-convergence argument: the regularization $\Phi\circ\texttt{hermCLM}$ of a
  \texttt{TestFun'} is a genuine \texttt{TestFun} with the same constants
  (\texttt{TestFun'.herm}), and \texttt{coordD2}, \texttt{wirtSecond} and the generator are
  unchanged by it at Hermitian points -- the assertion \texttt{Generator.lean} made in prose and
  never proved.

  \emph{A limit that is not cosmetic}: \texttt{TestFun'} still cannot be built for the raw,
  unregularized $\Phi$.  Its first- and second-order fields want the \emph{full} operator norm
  $\|\mathrm{fderiv}\,\Phi\,M\|$, while regularization only bounds
  $\|\mathrm{fderiv}\,\Phi\,M\circ P\|$, and anti-Hermitian directions are killed by $P$; writing
  the converse down produced a false statement that Lean rejected at once.  So the usable shape
  is \texttt{hasDerivAt\_integral\_Phi\_pairs\_of\_herm}, which asks only for
  \texttt{ContDiffAt} at Hermitian points plus the old class for the regularization -- both of
  which the real loop observables have.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Differentiate under the integral on $s\in(u/2,\infty)$; the integrand's derivative is
  $(2\sqrt s)^{-1}\sum_\alpha\omega_\alpha\partial_\alpha\Phi$; Stein turns $\omega_\alpha$ into
  $\mathrm{gvar}_\alpha\partial_\alpha$ and the $\sqrt u$ cancels.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Reverse bridge $\prec\Rightarrow$ moments, and the deterministic envelope]\label{lem:envelope}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.unifDetDom_integral_of_stochDom, RBM.Gauss.unifDetDom_integral_of_stochDom_of_nonneg, RBM.Gauss.momentDom_of_stochDom, RBM.Gauss.momentDom_of_stochDom_of_nonneg, RBM.Gauss.momentDom_of_normStochDom, RBM.Gauss.abs_im_zt, RBM.Gauss.norm_green_zt_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_gloop_le_det, RBM.Gauss.loopMax_le_det, RBM.Gauss.loopXi_le_det, RBM.Gauss.norm_gloop_sub_le_det, RBM.Gauss.det_envelope_le_rpow, RBM.Gauss.norm_gloop_le_rpow}
  \leanok\uses{lem:moment-dom, def:2.1-i}
  With a deterministic envelope of polynomial growth and $N^{-B}\le\Phi$, $Y\prec\Phi$ gives
  $\mathbb E|Y|^{2p}\le C_p N^{\varepsilon p}\Phi^{2p}$; together with the forward bridge the
  $\prec$-in/$\prec$-out interfaces keep their signatures. The envelope is free:
  $\operatorname{Im}z_t=\eta_t>0$ gives $\|G_t\|\le\eta_t^{-1}$ everywhere, so every $n$-loop obeys
  $\|L\|\le\eta_t^{-n}W^{-n+1}$ pointwise.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Split at $N^\tau\Phi$: the main part is $N^{2p\tau}\Phi^{2p}$, the exceptional part has probability
  $N^{-D'}$ and is controlled by the envelope with $D'=2p(K+B)+1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lemma 2.11 in moment form: the hierarchy for the derivative of the expected loop]\label{thm:moment-hierarchy}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.sample_G, RBM.Gauss.sample_Lval, RBM.Gauss.sample_ELval, RBM.Gauss.continuous_green_comp, RBM.Gauss.continuous_gloop_Hflow, RBM.Gauss.integrable_gloop_Hflow, RBM.Gauss.integrable_sample_Lval, RBM.Gauss.norm_sample_Lval_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_sample_ELval_le, RBM.Gauss.loopObs, RBM.Gauss.loopObs_Hflow, RBM.Gauss.norm_loopObs_le, RBM.Gauss.testFun_loopObs, RBM.Gauss.integrable_wirtSecond, RBM.Gauss.integral_sum_wirtSecond, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_integral_gloop, RBM.Gauss.LoopIto, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_integral_gloop_hierarchy, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_integral_Lval_hierarchy, RBM.Gauss.eGterm}
  \leanok\uses{thm:generator, def:gauss-model, lem:envelope}
  $\partial_v\mathbb E[L(H_v,z)]|_{v=u}=\mathbb E[\tilde E]+\mathbb E[\mathrm{primRhs}\,L_u]$, i.e.
  (2.45) in expectation, with the martingale term (2.46) simply absent. The SDE itself is never
  proved and never needed. $\mathbb E\,L_{u,\sigma,a}$ is a genuine Bochner integral
  (\texttt{integrable\_sample\_Lval}).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  The generator identity at the loop observable, precomposed with the Hermitian projection; the
  deterministic cut-and-glue algebra of Lemma 2.11 is carried as the hypothesis \texttt{LoopIto}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Moment Gr\"onwall: the second-order term is the quadratic variation]\label{thm:moment-gronwall}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.wirtFirst, RBM.Gauss.EmartCoeff, RBM.Gauss.quadVar, RBM.Gauss.quadVarPairs, RBM.Gauss.secondOrder_eq_quadVar, RBM.Gauss.momentFun, RBM.Gauss.momentFun_eq, RBM.Gauss.fderiv_momentFun, RBM.Gauss.fderiv2_momentFun, RBM.Gauss.genD, RBM.Gauss.genMomentPt, RBM.Gauss.genMomentPt_le, RBM.Gauss.genMomentPt_le_quadVarPairs, RBM.Gauss.momentIntegral, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_momentIntegral, RBM.Gauss.integral_genMomentPt_le, RBM.Gauss.le_gronwallBound_of_hasDerivWithinAt_le, RBM.Gauss.momentIntegral_le_gronwallBound, RBM.Gauss.BddC2, RBM.Gauss.bddC2_momentFun, RBM.Gauss.TestFun.of_bddC2, RBM.Gauss.resH, RBM.Gauss.norm_resH_le, RBM.Gauss.fderiv_resH_apply, RBM.Gauss.fderiv2_resH_apply, RBM.Gauss.greenObs, RBM.Gauss.bddC2_greenObs, RBM.Gauss.testFun_momentFun_greenObs, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_momentIntegral_greenObs, RBM.Gauss.contDiffAt_momentFun, RBM.Gauss.momentFun_hermFun, RBM.Gauss.genMomentPt_hermFun, RBM.Gauss.genMomentPt_le'}
  \leanok\uses{thm:generator, lem:envelope, lem:moment-dom}
  $\partial_u\mathbb E|F(H_u)|^{2p}=\mathbb E[\mathcal L(|F|^{2p})]$ with
  $\mathcal L(|F|^{2p})\le 2p\|F\|^{2p-1}\|\mathcal D F\|+p(2p-1)\|F\|^{2p-2}\sum_\alpha S_\alpha
  \|\partial_\alpha F\|^2$, and \texttt{secondOrder\_eq\_quadVar}:
  $\sum_{\alpha}S_\alpha\|\partial_\alpha F\|^2=\sum_{ij}|E^{(M)}_{ij}|^2$, the quadratic variation
  of (5.25) before its Schwarz split. Gr\"onwall closes it; \texttt{TestFun} is discharged for
  observables $\varphi(G)$.  The pointwise bound also holds in the Hermitian-only form
  \texttt{genMomentPt\_le'}, whose hypothesis is \texttt{ContDiffAt} at each Hermitian point
  rather than global $C^2$; that is the shape the moment Duhamel route can actually supply.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  The parallelogram law supplies the factor 2 between the ordered-pair sum weighted $S_{ij}$ and the
  coordinate sum weighted $S_{ij}/2$; the resolvent's global bounds $\eta^{-1},\eta^{-2},2\eta^{-3}$
  give the $C^2$ bounds.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Step 2 without the stopping time: continuous induction on moments]\label{thm:step2-moment}
  \lean{RBM.Step2Moment.le_of_bootstrap_weight, RBM.Step2Moment.ratR, RBM.Step2Moment.continuousOn_ratR, RBM.Step2Moment.jSnorm, RBM.Step2Moment.phi, RBM.Step2Moment.phiN, RBM.Step2Moment.continuousOn_phi, RBM.Step2Moment.continuousOn_phiN, RBM.Step2Moment.netTime, RBM.Step2Moment.exists_netTime_close, RBM.Step2Moment.stochDom_timeIcc_of_holder, RBM.Step2Moment.MomentHyp, RBM.Step2Moment.jSnorm_le_env, RBM.Step2Moment.integrable_jSnorm_pow, RBM.Step2Moment.phi_le, RBM.Step2Moment.phiN_le, RBM.Step2Moment.moment_bound, RBM.Step2Moment.stochDom_jSnorm, RBM.Step2Moment.jS_stochDom, RBM.Step2Moment.aprioriDecay_of_jS, RBM.Step2Moment.aprioriDecay, RBM.Step2Moment.step2}
  \leanok\uses{thm:step2, lem:moment-dom, lem:envelope, thm:moment-gronwall}
  $\varphi(u)=\mathbb E[(J^*_{u,D})^q]$ is deterministic and continuous, so the bootstrap of \S5.3
  closes by continuous induction: (5.43) and optional stopping are not used. The conclusions
  \texttt{jS\_stochDom}, \texttt{aprioriDecay} and \texttt{step2} are term-for-term those of T61.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Continuity of $\varphi$ by dominated convergence off the deterministic envelope; the prefix
  bootstrap applied to $\varphi/w$; the uncountable union over $u$ by the $N^{-C}$ net on the
  $N$-dependent interval $[s_N,t_N]$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Derivative bounds for the smooth truncation: every loss it costs is $N^{o(1)}$]\label{lem:cutoff-bounds}
  \lean{RBM.Cutoff.smoothMax, RBM.Cutoff.smoothMax_nonneg, RBM.Cutoff.le_smoothMax, RBM.Cutoff.smoothMax_le, RBM.Cutoff.rpow_card_le_exp_one, RBM.Cutoff.sum_rpow_sub_one_le, RBM.Cutoff.cutWeight, RBM.Cutoff.cutWeight_nonneg, RBM.Cutoff.sum_cutWeight_le, RBM.Cutoff.hasDerivAt_smoothMax, RBM.Cutoff.abs_sum_cutWeight_mul_le, RBM.Cutoff.sq_sum_weighted_le, RBM.Cutoff.sum_weighted_quadForm_le, RBM.Cutoff.sum_cutWeight_quadForm_le, RBM.Cutoff.logDerivA, RBM.Cutoff.hasDerivAt_tailT_flow, RBM.Cutoff.neg_deriv_tailT_le, RBM.Cutoff.neg_deriv_tailT_div_le, RBM.Cutoff.tailT_le_tailT_of_flow, RBM.Cutoff.one_le_rpow_mul_tailT_sq, RBM.Cutoff.inv_tailT_sq_le, RBM.Cutoff.sum_cutWeight_quadForm_tailT_le, RBM.Cutoff.ee_shape, RBM.Cutoff.hasDerivAt_threshold, RBM.Cutoff.hasDerivAt_cutComp, RBM.Cutoff.abs_threshold_drift_le, RBM.Cutoff.hasDerivAt_deriv_smoothMax, RBM.Cutoff.smoothMax_second_le}
  \leanok\uses{thm:step2-moment, lem:stretched-exp, lem:5.7b}
  The smooth truncation that the moment route puts where \S5.3 has a stopping time, with every
  cost it incurs \emph{quantified} rather than assumed -- and every one of them is $N^{o(1)}$.
  The $\ell^r$ smoothing of $\max_a$ costs $(\#\iota)^{1/r}$, which is at most $e$ as soon as
  $r\ge\log\#\iota$ (\texttt{rpow\_card\_le\_exp\_one}) -- this is the step that makes
  $r\asymp\log N$ give $O(1)$; the sum of the weights is bounded by H\"older
  (\texttt{sum\_cutWeight\_le}), and the chain rule \texttt{hasDerivAt\_smoothMax} is a theorem,
  not a hypothesis.  The second derivative is an \emph{identity}
  (\texttt{hasDerivAt\_deriv\_smoothMax}) whose third term has a favourable sign, so with
  $a_i\ge1$ -- which is (5.28)'s $J\ge1$ -- smoothing at second order costs exactly one factor
  $r-1\asymp\log N$.

  Two points where the expected extra work turned out not to be needed.  In $\partial_uT_{u,D}$
  the second term is genuinely not bounded uniformly in $\ell$, but its sign is favourable when
  $\ell'_u\ge0$ and it may simply be dropped, leaving the $\ell$-uniform
  $-\partial_u\log T\le2(\partial_u\log A_u)_+$.  And the $W^{-D}$ floor region needs \emph{no}
  separate treatment: the floor does not depend on $u$, so it contributes nothing to $T'$ and
  enters only through $(T-W^{-D})/T\le1$, which the same bound already covers.  The remaining
  piece, the time dependence of the threshold, is
  $\dot\Theta_u/\Theta_u=4m/\eta_u$ (\texttt{hasDerivAt\_threshold}), so on the gap
  $J/\Theta\in[1,2]$ it is $\le8C_\chi m/\eta_u$ -- the same order $\eta_u^{-1}$ as the other
  drift terms, and not a new obstruction.  For $\sum_xS_x(\partial_xJ)^2$ normalized by
  $T_{u,D}$, weighted Cauchy--Schwarz gives \texttt{sum\_cutWeight\_quadForm\_le} and the floor
  absorbs the additive remainder that is not proportional to $T^2$, at cost $W^{2D}$.

  \emph{Boundaries, stated as they are.}  (5.36) is connected by \emph{shape}
  (\texttt{ee\_shape} rearranges \ref{lem:5.7b}'s conclusion into $B\,T^2+R$) and not by applying
  it: the wiring is a one-line \texttt{linarith} but has not been done.  The second derivative
  here is a one-dimensional directional derivative, not a Fr\'echet Hessian -- enough for
  $\mathcal L=\tfrac12\sum S_{ij}\partial_{ij}\partial_{ji}$, which is a sum of directional second
  derivatives, but a consumer wanting the Fr\'echet form needs a wrapper.  No concrete $\chi$
  lives here: every statement about it is under the hypotheses $\mathrm{HasDerivAt}\,\chi$ and
  $|\chi'|\le C_\chi$, and the file contains \emph{no} pointwise quantifier over $\omega$
  anywhere, deliberately; the concrete $\chi$ is in \ref{thm:step2-momentstep}.  Removing the
  truncation again -- the crossing argument and the time net -- is by design not here.  Finally,
  this node gives only what each step loses ($e$, $e^2$, $r-1$, $W^{2D}$, $8C_\chi m/\eta$); the
  exponent budget itself is recomputed in \ref{thm:step2-momentstep}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  A self-check guards the weight exponent: at $a\equiv1$ and $\iota=\mathrm{Fin}\,2$ the weight sum
  is \emph{exactly} $2^{1/r}$, so a mistyped exponent fails to compile.  The constants of the
  globally bounded $C^2$ class are deliberately avoided here -- they carry no $T_{u,D}$
  normalization and would be off by a power of $A$, which the file header records.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The one-step improvement of Step 2 in moment form: the power counting, (5.48), and why \texttt{MomentHyp.step} is the wrong target]\label{thm:step2-momentstep}
  \lean{RBM.Step2MomentStep.cStep', RBM.Step2MomentStep.phi_arith', RBM.Step2MomentStep.hq_of_ratio, RBM.Step2MomentStep.hbeta_of_reg, RBM.Step2MomentStep.hgamma_of_reg, RBM.Step2MomentStep.beta_star_margin, RBM.Step2MomentStep.side_conditions_of_reg, RBM.Step2MomentStep.nearInt, RBM.Step2MomentStep.nearInt_le, RBM.Step2MomentStep.integral_nearInt_le, RBM.Step2MomentStep.coarse_sq_ge, RBM.Step2MomentStep.phi_lt_threshold, RBM.Step2MomentStep.le_of_gap_crossing, RBM.Step2MomentStep.netSet, RBM.Step2MomentStep.exists_netPoint_le, RBM.Step2MomentStep.le_of_gap_net, RBM.Step2MomentStep.hclose_of_modulus, RBM.Step2MomentStep.bootPP_step_of_net, RBM.Step2MomentStep.bootPP_of_net, RBM.Step2MomentStep.bootPP_of_modulus, RBM.Step2MomentStep.xiLK_two_apriori, RBM.Step2MomentStep.no_finite_pass, RBM.Step2MomentStep.linearize_on_support, RBM.Step2MomentStep.eq548_of_near_far, RBM.Step2MomentStep.flowEq548_of_near_far, RBM.Step2MomentStep.bnd_poly_excludes_pow, RBM.Step2MomentStep.detrunc_remainder_ge_thr, RBM.Step2MomentStep.detrunc_order_needed, RBM.Step2MomentStep.sat_phi_arith', RBM.Step2MomentStep.sat_hbeta_sharp, RBM.Step2MomentStep.sat_hgamma_sharp, RBM.Step2MomentStep.sat_le_of_gap_net, RBM.Step2MomentStep.sat_hclose, RBM.Step2MomentStep.sat_no_finite_pass, RBM.Step2MomentStep.sat_detrunc_sharp, RBM.Cutoff.hasDerivAt_maxPow, RBM.Cutoff.cutChi, RBM.Cutoff.cutChiD, RBM.Cutoff.cutChiDD, RBM.Cutoff.hasDerivAt_cutChi, RBM.Cutoff.hasDerivAt_cutChiD, RBM.Cutoff.cutChi_eq_one, RBM.Cutoff.cutChi_eq_zero, RBM.Cutoff.cutChi_antitone, RBM.Cutoff.contDiff_cutChi, RBM.Cutoff.abs_cutChiD_le, RBM.Cutoff.abs_cutChiDD_le, RBM.Cutoff.cutChiD_three_halves, RBM.Cutoff.hasDerivAt_cutComp_cutChi, RBM.Cutoff.abs_threshold_drift_cutChi_le, RBM.Cutoff.cutChi_spec}
  \leanok\uses{thm:step2-moment, thm:step2pp, thm:step45, lem:5.7a, lem:5.7b, lem:momentduhamelgauss, lem:cutoff-bounds}
  Two of the three deliverables land.  \texttt{BootPP.step}: the quantifier over the continuum of
  times is reduced to finitely many fixed times on a net (\texttt{bootPP\_step\_of\_net}), and
  \texttt{bootPP\_of\_net} / \texttt{bootPP\_of\_modulus} build the whole \texttt{BootPP}, the
  only thing left undischarged being the conditional improvement \emph{at the net points}.  And
  (5.48): \texttt{flowEq548\_of\_near\_far} produces \texttt{Step45.FlowEq548} outright, its near
  field being the sharpened (5.47), with only the far field \texttt{hfar} left.

  \emph{The power counting is compiled, not asserted.}  Taking shape 2 and skipping the
  (5.36)$\to$(5.42) absorption: both lines of (5.40) give $\beta^*=10$ (the bottleneck), the near
  field $q=r^3$ gives $\beta^*=4$ with zero margin -- it eats the $R^4$ budget exactly -- and the
  far field gives $5.5$ and $4.5$; all are below the $30$ of (2.72), whose exponent is untouched.
  \texttt{beta\_star\_margin} checks that the regularity hypothesis implies the side conditions as
  soon as $4\delta\le2c$, an order of magnitude inside $\delta\le c/24$.  The near-field integral
  had to be done before, not after, taking the supremum: \texttt{integral\_nearInt\_le} multiplies
  the four factors first and loses nothing in $R$, and \texttt{coarse\_sq\_ge} compiles the
  statement that the factorwise supremum overshoots by $R^{5/2}$.

  \emph{\texttt{MomentHyp.step} is unprovable in its frozen shape, and both obstructions are
  theorems.}  First, \texttt{bnd\_poly} forbids the bound from carrying any power of $N$
  (\texttt{bnd\_poly\_excludes\_pow}), while $J^*$ is a maximum over $L^2$ loops whose only
  elementary moment bound is the union bound, costing $L^2\asymp N^2$ at every order; removing
  that would need $\|\max\|_q\le L^{2/Q}\max\|Y_a\|_Q$ with $Q\gg q$, and the prefix hypothesis
  only offers the same $q$.  Second, the remainder from removing a truncation at the level of
  moments is not smaller than the threshold itself
  (\texttt{detrunc\_remainder\_ge\_thr}); pushing it down multiplies the order by
  $(K+\delta)/\delta$ (\texttt{detrunc\_order\_needed}), which on the flow is astronomical.  The
  frozen signature is therefore left alone and the replacement route is the gap crossing on a net
  (\texttt{le\_of\_gap\_crossing}, \texttt{le\_of\_gap\_net}), where the gap condition is needed
  only at net points and only conditionally on the prefix.  Separately, the shortcut of using the
  unconditional a priori bound of (2.73) to skip the $(+,+)$ bootstrap is \emph{refuted}:
  \texttt{xiLK\_two\_apriori} gives only $\Xi^{(L-K)}_{u,2}\prec A_u(R+C)$ (note the two
  normalizations in (5.76) differ by one $A$), and \texttt{no\_finite\_pass} shows the
  self-quadratic term of (5.83) makes $\theta\mapsto c+\theta^2A_t^{-1}$ non-contracting at
  $\theta\ge A_t$, so no fixed finite number of $\prec$-iterations reaches $\Theta=A_s^{1/2}$.

  \emph{The concrete cutoff.}  The smooth truncation the moment route substitutes for the
  stopping time is the explicit quintic Hermite $\chi$: identically $1$ on $[0,1]$, identically
  $0$ on $[2,\infty)$, non-increasing, exactly $C^2$ -- which is what the generator needs -- with
  $\|\chi'\|_\infty\le15/8$ (attained at $3/2$, so tight) and $\|\chi''\|_\infty\le15$.  Mathlib's
  \texttt{Real.smoothTransition} and \texttt{ContDiffBump} cannot serve: they carry no derivative
  formula and no derivative bound, and the threshold drift estimate needs an actual number.
  \texttt{cutChi\_spec} bundles the whole list of requirements into one inhabited statement, so
  that they are not jointly unsatisfiable, and numerical self-checks make a mistyped coefficient a
  compile error.

  \emph{Still open, with owners.}  The conditional improvement \texttt{hev} at the net points and
  the replacement structure for \texttt{MomentHyp} both need a \emph{truncated} moment Duhamel
  interface, which \ref{thm:momentduhamel} does not have; and \texttt{hfar} needs the three
  indicator functions of (5.39)/(5.41)/(5.44) to survive a one-step bound, which the existing
  one-step bound discards (paper-deltas \#130).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Degenerate-point witnesses make every satisfiability claim a theorem rather than a comment: the
  hypotheses of \texttt{phi\_arith'} are simultaneously satisfiable with a non-vacuous conclusion,
  the side conditions behind $5.5$ and $4.5$ are attained at $r=R=x=A=1$ so those exponents cannot
  be lowered by this argument, and both negative results are attained as well.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Definition 5.4, (5.22), (5.25), and Lemma 5.5 without BDG]\label{thm:discharge-bdg}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.emart, RBM.Gauss.eeRaw, RBM.Gauss.eeRaw_self_eq_quadVar, RBM.Gauss.loopCut, RBM.Gauss.emartEdge, RBM.Gauss.eeEdge, RBM.Gauss.eeTens, RBM.Gauss.eeEdge_self, RBM.Gauss.sum_Sblk_mul_conj, RBM.Gauss.eeEdge_eq_sum_SB, RBM.Gauss.toIdx, RBM.Gauss.EmartCoeff_sum, RBM.Gauss.emart_Uker, RBM.Gauss.quadVarPairs_Uker, RBM.Gauss.quadVarPairs_le_of_split, RBM.Gauss.genMomentPt_le_driftless, RBM.Gauss.integrable_quadVar_pow, RBM.Gauss.momentIntegral_le_of_quadVar, RBM.Gauss.momentIntegral_le_exp, RBM.Gauss.momentDom_of_quadVar, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_of_quadVar}
  \leanok\uses{thm:moment-gronwall, lem:envelope, lem:moment-dom, thm:moment-hierarchy}
  $E\otimes E$ of Definition 5.4 is given a definition, and its diagonal \emph{is} the quadratic
  variation (\texttt{eeRaw\_self\_eq\_quadVar}); (5.22) via $\sum_{ij}S_{ij}R_{ji}\overline{R'_{ji}}
  =W\sum_{bb'}S^{(B)}_{bb'}\langle E_{b'}RE_b R'^\dagger\rangle$; the Schwarz split (5.25); and
  Lemma 5.5's conclusion by Gr\"onwall, with ``this term is a martingale'' replaced by
  $\mathcal LF=0$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $U_{u,t,\sigma}$ is a deterministic linear combination, so the Wirtinger derivative passes through
  it (\texttt{emart\_Uker}); then moments in, Gr\"onwall, $\prec$ out.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Conditional expectation as row integration, and the minor replacement error]\label{lem:condrow}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.IsRowCoord, RBM.Gauss.rowSplit, RBM.Gauss.rowSplit_rowSplit, RBM.Gauss.measurePreserving_rowSplit, RBM.Gauss.condRow, RBM.Gauss.condRow_apply, RBM.Gauss.RowIntegrable, RBM.Gauss.condRow_condRow, RBM.Gauss.condRow_sub_condRow, RBM.Gauss.integral_condRow, RBM.Gauss.condRow_mul_of_finDepOffRow, RBM.Gauss.FinDepOffRow, RBM.Gauss.FinDepOffRow.rowSplit_eq, RBM.Gauss.FinDepOffRow.finDep, RBM.Gauss.condRow_of_finDepOffRow, RBM.Gauss.finDepOffRow_of_minor, RBM.Gauss.greenMinorMat, RBM.Gauss.finDepOffRow_greenMinorMat, RBM.Gauss.greenMinorMat_eq_minorGreen, RBM.Gauss.sub_smul_one_apply, RBM.Gauss.greenMinor_diag_sub, RBM.Gauss.norm_greenMinor_sub_le_sq, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_offdiag_of_localLaw, RBM.Gauss.minorReplace_indicator_stochDom, RBM.Gauss.minorReplace_stochDom, RBM.Gauss.minorReplace_diag_stochDom, RBM.Gauss.minorGreen_localLaw_indicator_stochDom, RBM.Gauss.minorGreen_localLaw_stochDom, RBM.Gauss.minorGreen_localLaw_diag_stochDom}
  \leanok\uses{def:gauss-model, lem:4.2, lem:4.1}
  In the product model $\mathbb E_k[\cdot]$ is literally integration over the row-$k$ coordinates
  (exact Fubini, not \texttt{condExp}), so $\mathbb E_k\circ\mathbb E_k=\mathbb E_k$ and
  $\mathbb E_k\circ(1-\mathbb E_k)=0$ hold pointwise; $G^{(k)}$ is \texttt{FinDepOffRow}, reading no
  row-$k$ coordinate. With (4.9) and the local law, $|G_{ll}-G^{(k)}_{ll}|\prec\Psi^2$ for $l\ne k$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $(\omega,\omega')\mapsto\mathrm{rowSplit}$ pushes $P\otimes P$ to $P$; the replacement error is the
  exact identity $G^{(k)}_{ll}-G_{ll}=-G_{lk}G_{kl}/G_{kk}$ with $|G_{kk}|\ge1/2$ read off (4.1).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Vanishing lemma: an index occurring once kills the expectation]\label{lem:fluc-vanish}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.finDepOffRow_const, RBM.Gauss.FinDepOffRow.mul, RBM.Gauss.FinDepOffRow.sub, RBM.Gauss.finDepOffRow_prod, RBM.Gauss.finDepOffRow_condRow, RBM.Gauss.norm_prod_sub_prod_le, RBM.Gauss.integral_mul_prod_eq_zero, RBM.Gauss.norm_integral_prod_le, RBM.Gauss.greenDiagCentered, RBM.Gauss.flucDiag, RBM.Gauss.flucDiagMinor, RBM.Gauss.flucDiagMinorFam, RBM.Gauss.condRow_flucDiag, RBM.Gauss.finDepOffRow_flucDiagMinorFam, RBM.Gauss.norm_flucDiag_sub_flucDiagMinorFam_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_flucDiag_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_flucDiagMinorFam_le, RBM.Gauss.greenMinorMat_apply_eq_greenMinor, RBM.Gauss.norm_integral_prod_flucDiag_le}
  \leanok\uses{lem:condrow, lem:4.1}
  With $Z_k=(1-\mathbb E_k)(G_{kk}-m)$, if $k_{i_0}$ occurs exactly once in
  $\prod_i Z_{k_i}$ then $\|\mathbb E\prod_i Z_{k_i}\|\le(\#\iota-1)\,\varepsilon\,B^{\#\iota-1}$,
  where $\varepsilon$ is the replacement error and $B$ bounds the factors.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Replace every other factor by its $G^{(k_{i_0})}$ version; those read no row $k_{i_0}$, so
  $\mathbb E_{k_{i_0}}$ passes through and annihilates $Z_{k_{i_0}}$, giving exactly $0$; the
  replacement error is telescoped by \texttt{norm\_prod\_sub\_prod\_le}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Counting: stratifying the moment expansion by lone slots]\label{lem:fluc-count}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.HasLoneSlot, RBM.Gauss.two_le_card_fiber, RBM.Gauss.two_mul_card_image_le, RBM.Gauss.card_filter_not_hasLoneSlot_le, RBM.Gauss.UniformWeight, RBM.Gauss.UniformWeight.sum_abs_le, RBM.Gauss.sum_prod_not_hasLoneSlot_le, RBM.Gauss.epsHom, RBM.Gauss.prod_epsHom, RBM.Gauss.prod_epsHom_sum_eq, RBM.Gauss.condRow_epsHom, RBM.Gauss.flucAvg, RBM.Gauss.integral_norm_flucAvg_pow_le, RBM.Gauss.integral_norm_flucAvg_pow_le_uniform, RBM.Gauss.uniformWeight_blockAvg, RBM.Gauss.uniformWeight_Sblk}
  \leanok\uses{lem:fluc-vanish, lem:condrow}
  Expanding $\mathbb E|\sum_k t_kZ_k|^{2p}$ over multi-indices and splitting on whether some slot's
  index occurs once: the lone stratum dies by the vanishing lemma, and in the complement every index
  occurs twice, so at most $p$ are distinct and the weights sum to $c^p p^{2p}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Slots indexed by $\mathrm{Fin}\,p\oplus\mathrm{Fin}\,p$ so conjugation is a case split;
  $2\,\#\mathrm{image}\,v\le\#\iota$ gives the count.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Linear LDE: the Gaussian row and column sums]\label{lem:lde-linear}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.rowSet, RBM.Gauss.indepFun_rowSet, RBM.Gauss.offRowCoord,
    RBM.Gauss.Hflow_submatrix_congr_offRowCoord, RBM.Gauss.rowCoord, RBM.Gauss.Xentry_eq_rowCoord,
    RBM.Gauss.re_row_sum, RBM.Gauss.im_row_sum, RBM.Gauss.integral_norm_row_sum_pow_le,
    RBM.Gauss.rowSum, RBM.Gauss.rowVarSum, RBM.Gauss.integral_norm_rowSum_pow_le,
    RBM.Gauss.rowCoeffNorm, RBM.Gauss.rowVarSum_rowCoeffNorm,
    RBM.Gauss.lintegral_norm_rowSum_norm_pow_le, RBM.Gauss.integrable_norm_rowSum_norm_pow,
    RBM.Gauss.integral_norm_rowSum_norm_pow_le', RBM.Gauss.minorCol, RBM.Gauss.minorCol_congr,
    RBM.Gauss.measurable_minorCol, RBM.Gauss.minorCol_eq_greenMinor, RBM.Gauss.ldeRowLHS_eq,
    RBM.Gauss.rowVarSum_eq, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_rowSum_general,
    RBM.Gauss.stochDom_rowSum_minorCol, RBM.Gauss.minorRowConj,
    RBM.Gauss.stochDom_rowSum_minorRowConj,
    RBM.Gauss.lintegral_norm_rowSum_pow_le_of_const,
    RBM.Gauss.rowSum_ae_eq_zero_of_varSum_eq_zero, RBM.Gauss.rowSum_rowCoeffNorm,
    RBM.Gauss.highProb_norm_rowSum_sq_le, RBM.Gauss.eventually_card_LdeIdx_le,
    RBM.im_green_diag, RBM.green_diag_ne_zero, RBM.Gauss.isUnit_det_Hflow_sub,
    RBM.Gauss.green_Hflow_diag_ne_zero, RBM.Gauss.minorRowConj_eq_greenMinor,
    RBM.Gauss.ldeColLHS_eq, RBM.Gauss.rowVarSum_minorRowConj_eq,
    RBM.Gauss.stochDom_sq_of_rowSum, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_ldeRow, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_ldeCol}
  \leanok\uses{def:gauss-model, lem:moment-dom}
  For the Gaussian model, $\bigl|\sum_{k\ne i}H_{ik}G^{(i)}_{kj}\bigr|
  \prec\bigl(\sum_k S_{ik}|G^{(i)}_{kj}|^2\bigr)^{1/2}$ uniformly in $(i,j)$, and the same for the
  column sums: the $\mathtt{LDERow}$ and $\mathtt{LDECol}$ hypotheses of
  \texttt{Green/EntryBound.lean} hold for the Gaussian ensemble.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok\uses{def:gauss-model, lem:moment-dom}
  $G^{(i)}$ is the resolvent of the minor, hence a function of the coordinates off row $i$, which
  are independent of the row.  Conditionally on them the row sum is a centred complex Gaussian:
  its real and imaginary parts are real linear forms in the row coordinates, with equal variances
  $\tfrac12 u\sum_kS_{ik}|c_k|^2$.  The Gaussian even moments give
  $\mathbb E\|Z\|^{2p}\le2(2p-1)!!\,(u\sum_kS_{ik}|c_k|^2)^p$ fibrewise; normalising by the
  conditional standard deviation makes the bound constant, and Tonelli across the two blocks
  removes every integrability side condition.  The union bound over the $\le(LW)^2\le N^2$ pairs
  is \texttt{stochDom\_of\_momentDom}.  The column case is the conjugate row sum, by Hermitian
  symmetry.

  To land on the hypotheses of \texttt{entry\_bound\_stochDom} three gaps are closed.  The
  normalised bound says nothing where the conditional variance vanishes, while the LDE
  inequality demands that the sum itself vanish there: it does, almost surely.  The
  identification of $\mathtt{ldeRowLHS}$ with the row sum needs $H-z$ invertible and
  $G_{ii}\ne0$; both hold for \emph{every} $\omega$ when $\operatorname{Im}z\ne0$, the second by
  the one-site Ward identity $\operatorname{Im}G_{ii}=\operatorname{Im}z\,\|Ge_i\|^2$.  Finally
  the variance carries a factor $u$, harmless for $u\le1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Gaussian integration by parts against polynomially bounded integrands]\label{lem:gauss-ibp}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.add_pow_le_two_pow_mul, RBM.Gauss.integrable_abs_pow_coord,
    RBM.Gauss.continuous_polyW, RBM.Gauss.integrable_polyW_pow,
    RBM.Gauss.integrable_abs_pow_gaussianReal, RBM.Gauss.integral_mul_gaussianReal_int,
    RBM.Gauss.integral_mul_gaussianReal_complex_int, RBM.Gauss.polyW_upd_le,
    RBM.Gauss.norm_le_of_tame_upd, RBM.Gauss.gaussIBP}
  \leanok\uses{def:gauss-model}
  $\mathbb E[\omega_c\,g]=\sigma_c\,\mathbb E[\partial_c g]$ for every continuous, finitely
  dependent, \emph{polynomially} bounded $g$, and every polynomial moment of the Gaussian
  product measure is finite.  This discharges the structure \texttt{GaussIBP} carried by the
  quadratic estimate.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  No smooth cutoff is needed.  The one-dimensional identity
  \texttt{integral\_mul\_gaussianReal} already takes \emph{integrability} hypotheses rather
  than boundedness --- the matrix version of T70 merely used its bounded corollary --- so the
  same fibrewise argument goes through: resampling the coordinate $c$ leaves the measure
  invariant, Fubini separates it, and on the fibre the integrand is polynomially bounded, hence
  integrable.  Polynomial integrability itself is an induction on the finite coordinate set,
  using $(a+b)^n\le2^n(a^n+b^n)$ and the one-coordinate Gaussian moments.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Quadratic LDE: Gaussian Hanson--Wright by moment recursion]\label{lem:lde-quad}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.polyW, RBM.Gauss.Tame, RBM.Gauss.Tame.integrable, RBM.Gauss.young_pow, RBM.Gauss.GaussIBP, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.sum_coord_mul_deriv, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.integral_chaos_mul, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.moment_recursion, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.two_mul_mom_succ_le, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.mom_succ_le, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.integral_conj_h_mul_h, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.momT_zero, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.mom_one, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.norm_chaos_sq_eq_ldeQuadLHS, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.Vq_eq_ldeQuadRHS, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.integral_ldeQuadLHS_eq}
  \leanok\uses{def:gauss-model, thm:generator, lem:condrow}
  One integration by parts per row coordinate turns $\mathbb E|Q|^{2p}$ into a recursion:
  $\mathbb E|Q|^{2p}\le(2p-1)^p\,\mathbb E[T^p]$, with the variance identity
  $\mathbb E|Q|^2=\mathbb E\sum_{k,l}\sigma_k\|B_{kl}\|^2\sigma_l$ exact. The two sides match
  \texttt{ldeQuadLHS}/\texttt{ldeQuadRHS} verbatim.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Euler's identity $\sum_\alpha\omega_\alpha\partial_\alpha Q=2(Q+\sum_k\sigma_kB_{kk})$, Stein per
  coordinate, and Young's inequality with a rational exponent close the recursion.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Positive chaos moment bound: the $p$-th moment of $T$ is controlled by that of $V_q$]\label{lem:lde-quad-T}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.wirtVal, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.wirtVal_U, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.wirtVal_V,
    RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.wirtVal_conj_U, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.wirtVal_conj_V,
    RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.integral_conj_h_mul_gen, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.TA, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.TB,
    RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.hasDerivAt_Tq_true, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.hasDerivAt_Tq_false,
    RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.wirtVal_TA_TB, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.Wt,
    RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.Tq_eq_sum_conj_h_mul, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.wirtVal_Wt,
    RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.WA, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.WB, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.hasDerivAt_Wt_true,
    RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.hasDerivAt_Wt_false, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.Zt, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.ZA,
    RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.ZB, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.Tq_pow_succ_eq_sum,
    RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.wirtVal_ZA_ZB, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.integral_Tq_pow_succ,
    RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.sum_sg_mul_normSq, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.sum_w_diag,
    RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.weighted_cauchy, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.Arow, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.sq_Arow_le,
    RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.crossT, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.sum_w_wirtVal_ZA_ZB,
    RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.norm_crossT_le, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.momVpow,
    RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.momTpow_succ_le, RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.momTpow_le,
    RBM.Gauss.RowChaos.mom_le_momVpow}
  \leanok\uses{lem:lde-quad, def:gauss-model}
  With $T=\sum_k\sigma_k(\|U_k\|^2+\|V_k\|^2)$ and
  $V_q=\sum_{k,l}\sigma_k\|B_{kl}\|^2\sigma_l$, one has
  $\mathbb E[T^{p}]\le(4p-2)^{p}\,\mathbb E[V_q^{p}]$, and hence, with
  \texttt{mom\_succ\_le}, $\mathbb E|Q|^{2p}\le\bigl((2p-1)(4p-2)\bigr)^{p}\mathbb E[V_q^p]$:
  the quadratic large deviation estimate in the paper's own control.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Conditioning-free, so no conditional expectation is needed.  Both $U_k\bar U_k$ and
  $V_k\bar V_k$ carry exactly one factor $\bar h$, so $T=\sum_l\bar h_lW_l$ with
  $W_l=\sum_k\sigma_k(B_{kl}\bar U_k+V_k\bar B_{lk})$, and $T^{q+1}=\sum_l\bar h_lW_lT^q$.
  Gaussian integration by parts along the row entry gives
  $\int\bar h_lZ=w_l\int D_lZ$ for the Wirtinger derivation
  $D_l=r(\partial_{a_l}-\varepsilon_l i\,\partial_{b_l})$, which satisfies
  $D_lU_k=0$, $D_l\bar V_k=0$, $D_l\bar U_k=2r^2\bar B_{kl}$, $D_lV_k=2r^2B_{lk}$.  The
  diagonal part reproduces $2V_qT^q$ exactly; the cross term is bounded by Cauchy--Schwarz,
  first in $k$ and then against $\sigma_l$, by $4qV_qT^q$.  The resulting
  $\mathbb E[T^{q+1}]\le(4q+2)\mathbb E[V_qT^q]$ is closed by the same pointwise Young
  inequality \texttt{young\_pow} that closes \texttt{mom\_succ\_le}, with $K=4q+2$; every
  exponent stays a natural number.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The row chaos of the Gaussian model]\label{lem:lde-quad-inst}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.continuous_green_of_isHermitian, RBM.Gauss.minorRes,
    RBM.Gauss.isHermitian_Hflow_submatrix, RBM.Gauss.norm_minorRes_le,
    RBM.Gauss.continuous_minorRes, RBM.Gauss.minorRes_congr, RBM.Gauss.modelChaos,
    RBM.Gauss.modelChaos_h, RBM.Gauss.modelChaos_sg, RBM.Gauss.modelChaos_B_eq,
    RBM.Gauss.modelChaos_normSq_chaos, RBM.Gauss.modelChaos_Vq,
    RBM.Gauss.integral_ldeQuadLHS_pow_le}
  \leanok\uses{lem:lde-quad, lem:lde-quad-T, def:gauss-model, lem:4.1}
  The abstract row chaos is instantiated at the Gaussian model: at row $i$ the row is
  $h_k=(H_u)_{ik}$, the variance is $\sigma_k=uS_{ik}$ and the matrix is $G^{(i)}$.  Hence,
  for every $p\ge1$, $0\le u$ and $\operatorname{Im}z\ne0$,
  $\mathbb E[(\mathtt{ldeQuadLHS})^p]\le\bigl((2p-1)(4p-2)\bigr)^p u^{2p}\,
  \mathbb E[(\mathtt{ldeQuadRHS})^p]$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  \texttt{RowChaos} demands a \emph{globally} bounded, continuous matrix that reads no row
  coordinate.  $\mathtt{greenMinor}\,G\,i=G_{kl}-G_{ki}G_{il}/G_{ii}$ is not globally bounded,
  because $G_{ii}$ has no global lower bound.  Taking instead the minor resolvent
  $(H^{(i)}-z)^{-1}$ gives all three: it is bounded by $|\operatorname{Im}z|^{-1}$ (a submatrix
  of a Hermitian matrix is Hermitian), continuous off the real axis, and a function of the
  off-row block alone.  It coincides with $\mathtt{greenMinor}$ for \emph{every} $\omega$,
  since the two side conditions of \texttt{inv\_minor\_resolvent} --- invertibility and
  $G_{ii}\ne0$ --- hold unconditionally when $\operatorname{Im}z\ne0$.  The row data
  $\mathtt{co}/\mathtt{eps}$ is the parametrisation already built for the linear estimate.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The quadratic LDE as stochastic domination]\label{lem:lde-quad-dom}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.vqM, RBM.Gauss.sqVq, RBM.Gauss.modelChaosEps,
    RBM.Gauss.chaos_modelChaosEps, RBM.Gauss.Vq_modelChaosEps,
    RBM.Gauss.Vq_modelChaosEps_le_one, RBM.Gauss.hwConst, RBM.Gauss.mom_modelChaosEps_le,
    RBM.Gauss.norm_chaos_modelChaosEps, RBM.Gauss.meas_lt_normSq_chaos_le_eps,
    RBM.Gauss.meas_lt_normSq_chaos_le, RBM.Gauss.chaos_modelChaos_zero,
    RBM.Gauss.stochDom_ldeQuad}
  \leanok\uses{lem:lde-quad-inst, lem:lde-quad-T, lem:4.1}
  For the Gaussian flow with $0\le u\le1$ and $\operatorname{Im}z\ne0$,
  $\mathtt{ldeQuadLHS}\prec\mathtt{ldeQuadRHS}$ uniformly in $i$: the hypothesis
  \texttt{hLquad} of \texttt{diag\_bound\_stochDom}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  The moment bound has a \emph{random} control, which \texttt{stochDom\_of\_momentDom} does not
  accept, and Markov cannot be applied directly because the threshold $N^\tau\zeta(\omega)$ moves
  with $\omega$.  Rescaling the matrix by the off-row-measurable factor $(V_q+\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$
  --- constant in $(k,l)$, so it divides the chaos too --- makes the control $V_q/(V_q+\varepsilon)
  \le1$ and keeps the matrix globally bounded (by $\mathtt{Bbd}/\sqrt\varepsilon$), which is what
  \texttt{RowChaos} requires.  Hence
  $\mathbb E[(|Q|^2/(V_q+\varepsilon))^{p}]\le A_{p}$ for every $\varepsilon>0$, with $A_p$ free of
  $\varepsilon$, and Markov now has a deterministic threshold.  Finally
  $\{|Q|^2>\lambda V_q\}=\bigcup_n\{|Q|^2>\lambda(V_q+\tfrac1{n+1})\}$ is an increasing union, so
  continuity of the measure from below removes $\varepsilon$ --- with no integral limit theorem and
  no separate treatment of $\{V_q=0\}$.  The union bound over the $LW\le N$ sites and the choice
  $p$ large finish it; $u=0$ is separate, the chaos vanishing there.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Diagonal LDE: $|H_{ii}|^2\prec S_{ii}$]\label{lem:lde-diag}
  \lean{RBM.Sblk_diag_pos, RBM.Gauss.integral_pow_coord, RBM.Gauss.integrable_pow_coord,
    RBM.Gauss.normSq_Hflow_diag, RBM.Gauss.integral_norm_Hflow_diag_pow,
    RBM.Gauss.integrable_norm_Hflow_diag_pow, RBM.Gauss.eventually_card_Idx_le,
    RBM.Gauss.stochDom_normSq_Hflow_diag}
  \leanok\uses{def:gauss-model, lem:moment-dom}
  For the Gaussian flow, $|H_{ii}|^2\prec S_{ii}$ uniformly in $i$: the hypothesis
  \texttt{hLdiag} of \texttt{diag\_bound\_stochDom}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  The diagonal of a Hermitian matrix is real, and \texttt{Xentry} reads a single coordinate
  there, so $H_{ii}=\sqrt u\,\omega_{(i,i,\mathrm{tt})}$ is a one-dimensional centred Gaussian of
  variance $u S_{ii}$.  Hence $\mathbb E|H_{ii}|^{2p}=u^p(2p-1)!!\,S_{ii}^p$, and the factor
  $u^{2p}$ is absorbed into the constant of \texttt{MomentDom}, so no upper bound on $u$ is
  needed.  The control $S_{ii}=1/(3W)$ is strictly positive, and the union bound runs over the
  $LW\le N$ sites.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The Hölder modulus of the flow in the time variable]\label{lem:flow-holder}
  \lean{RBM.green_sub_eq, RBM.norm_green_sub_le, RBM.abs_sqrt_sub_sqrt_le, RBM.norm_zt_sub,
    RBM.Gauss.norm_green_flow_sub_le, RBM.Gauss.abs_llErr_sub_le, RBM.Gauss.abs_llMax_sub_le,
    RBM.Gauss.llMax_le_inv_etaT, RBM.Gauss.abs_llMax_sq_sub_le,
    RBM.Gauss.etaT_pos_of_lt_one', RBM.Gauss.etaT_le_of_le, RBM.Gauss.self_le_rpow_half,
    RBM.Gauss.abs_llMax_sq_sub_le_holder}
  \leanok\uses{def:gauss-model, lem:4.1-fixedtime}
  For $0\le u,u'\le t<1$ and $|u-u'|\le1$,
  $\bigl|\,\|G_u-m\|_{\max}^2-\|G_{u'}-m\|_{\max}^2\,\bigr|
  \le(2\eta_t^{-1}+2)\eta_t^{-2}(\|X\|+1)\,|u-u'|^{1/2}$.
  Purely deterministic; $\|X\|\prec1$ is not used.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Both the matrix and the spectral parameter move, so
  $G_u-G_{u'}=G_u\bigl((H_{u'}-z_{u'})-(H_u-z_u)\bigr)G_{u'}$.  The matrix part is
  $\|H_u-H_{u'}\|=|\sqrt u-\sqrt{u'}|\,\|X\|$, and the parameter part is simply
  $\|z_u-z_{u'}\|=|u-u'|$, because $z_t=E+(1-t)m(E)$ and $\|m(E)\|=1$.  Entrywise
  $\le$ operator norm transfers this to $\|G-m\|_{\max}$, and
  $|a^2-b^2|=(a+b)|a-b|$ with $\|G_u-m\|_{\max}\le\eta_u^{-1}+1$ to its square.  On $[0,t]$
  both $\eta_u^{-1}$ are at most $\eta_t^{-1}$, and
  $|\sqrt u-\sqrt{u'}|\le|u-u'|^{1/2}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Lemma 4.1 at a fixed time, in the language of the flow]\label{lem:4.1-fixedtime}
  \lean{RBM.norm_gloop_pm_eq_Lre, RBM.Sample.llErr_eq,
    RBM.Step1.goodEv_eq_setOf_goodEvent, RBM.StochDom.control_mono,
    RBM.StochDom.trans_indicator, RBM.Gauss.sample_Lval_pm, RBM.Gauss.goodEv_eq_goodSet,
    RBM.Gauss.LoopHyp, RBM.Gauss.loopHyp_iff, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_Lmax,
    RBM.Gauss.card_sbSupport_le, RBM.Gauss.sum_sum_Lre_le,
    RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_entryControl, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_diag,
    RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_offdiag, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_llMax_sq}
  \leanok\uses{lem:4.1-gauss, lem:4.1}
  For the Gaussian flow at a fixed time $u$: if $1_\Omega\|L_{u,(+,-),(a,b)}\|\prec\Phi$ with
  $\Phi$ deterministic, then $1_\Omega\|G_u-m\|_{\max}^2\prec\Phi+W^{-1}$.  This is
  \texttt{Lemma41Flow} with the time quantifier removed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  The two vocabularies agree: the $(+,-)$ two-loop is the nonnegative real
  $W^{-2}\sum|G|^2$, so its norm is $L^{re}$; \texttt{llErr} is the entrywise quantity of
  \texttt{GoodEvent}; and \texttt{goodEv} is \texttt{goodSet} at $\delta=(W\ell_u\eta_u)^{-1/6}$.
  The controls of (4.2) and (4.3) carry no indicator while the hypothesis does, but they chain
  anyway, because on the failure event the left-hand side is positive and so $\omega$ lies in
  $\Omega$.  Each of the at most nine two-loops in the control of (4.2) is at most $L^{\max}$,
  which is attained, and $\|G-m\|_{\max}^2>c$ forces some entry to exceed $\sqrt c$; the
  off-diagonal entries are covered by (4.2) and the diagonal ones by (4.3).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Lemma 4.1 for the Gaussian model, with no LDE hypotheses]\label{lem:4.1-gauss}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.entry_bound_gauss, RBM.Gauss.diag_bound_gauss}
  \leanok\uses{lem:4.1, lem:lde-linear, lem:lde-diag, lem:lde-quad-dom}
  For the Gaussian flow $H_u=\sqrt u\,X$, (4.2) and (4.3) hold with \emph{no} large deviation
  hypothesis: all four --- the row and column estimates of (4.8), the quadratic form of (4.7)
  and $|H_{ii}|^2\prec S_{ii}$ --- are theorems.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Instantiate \texttt{entry\_bound\_stochDom} and \texttt{diag\_bound\_stochDom} at
  $H=H_u$, feeding them \texttt{stochDom\_ldeRow}, \texttt{stochDom\_ldeCol},
  \texttt{stochDom\_ldeQuad} and \texttt{stochDom\_normSq\_Hflow\_diag}.  For (4.3) the
  spectral parameter is $z_t$, whose imaginary part is nonzero by $|E|\le2-\kappa$ and $t<1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Assembly of the fluctuation average: (4.12) into (4.5)]\label{lem:fluc-avg}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.measurable_matrix_inv_apply, RBM.Gauss.measurable_green_apply, RBM.Gauss.measurable_condRow, RBM.Gauss.measurable_flucDiag, RBM.Gauss.measurable_flucAvg, RBM.Gauss.norm_green_apply_le_etaT, RBM.Gauss.greenMinorMat_eq_green_submatrix, RBM.Gauss.norm_greenDiagCentered_le_env, RBM.Gauss.rowIntegrable_greenDiagCentered, RBM.Gauss.FlucBound, RBM.Gauss.flucBound_env, RBM.Gauss.integral_norm_flucAvg_pow_le_of_flucBound, RBM.Gauss.integrable_norm_flucAvg_pow, RBM.Gauss.momentDom_flucAvg, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_flucAvg, RBM.Gauss.card_blockAvg_support, RBM.Gauss.card_Sblk_support, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_flucAvg_blockAvg, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_flucAvg_Sblk, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_flucAvg_blockAvg_env, RBM.Gauss.condExpDiag, RBM.Gauss.flucAvg_eq_sum_sub, RBM.Gauss.norm_trace_green_sub_mul_Eblk_le_flucAvg, RBM.Gauss.trace_green_sub_mul_Eblk_stochDom}
  \leanok\uses{lem:fluc-count, lem:fluc-vanish, lem:moment-dom, lem:4.1}
  Measurability of $G$, of $\mathbb E_k$ and of the fluctuation average are proved; the deterministic
  envelope supplies genuine pointwise parameters, so the moment bound is unconditional and
  \texttt{hFA} is discharged at its existing signature, giving (4.5) from (4.12) and \texttt{hIBP}.
  (4.12) itself still needs the minor replacement iterated to order $2p$.
  \texttt{FlucBound} is satisfiable but is a \emph{ceiling}: its constant $B$ has an absolute
  lower bound, so this interface cannot carry (4.12) at the size $B\asymp\Psi$ the paper needs --
  see \ref{thm:eq412-cond} for the budgeted interfaces that can.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $A^{-1}=\mathrm{Ring.inverse}(\det A)\cdot\mathrm{adj}\,A$ for measurability;
  \texttt{StronglyMeasurable.integral\_prod\_right'} for $\mathbb E_k$;
  $\|G\|\le\eta_t^{-1}$ for the envelope.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[(4.12) by iterating the vanishing lemma to order $2p$]\label{thm:fluc-iter}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.predSplit, RBM.Gauss.measurePreserving_predSplit, RBM.Gauss.condPred, RBM.Gauss.condPred_condPred, RBM.Gauss.condRow_condRow_comm, RBM.Gauss.qRow, RBM.Gauss.applyOps, RBM.Gauss.numQ, RBM.Gauss.condRow_applyOps_qRow, RBM.Gauss.pivotFam, RBM.Gauss.prod_eq_sum_pivotFam, RBM.Gauss.integral_prod_pivotFam_empty, RBM.Gauss.OpsOkOut, RBM.Gauss.OpsOkOut.cons, RBM.Gauss.norm_integral_prod_applyOps_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_integral_prod_qRow_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_qRow_flucDiag_le, RBM.Gauss.FlucGain, RBM.Gauss.flucGain_env, RBM.Gauss.norm_integral_prod_epsHom_flucDiag_le, RBM.Gauss.loneSlots, RBM.Gauss.two_mul_card_image_le_add_card_loneSlots, RBM.Gauss.card_filter_card_image_le, RBM.Gauss.sum_weighted_le, RBM.Gauss.integral_norm_flucAvg_pow_le_iter, RBM.Gauss.FlucGainUpTo, RBM.Gauss.FlucGain.upTo, RBM.Gauss.OpsOkOut.length_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_integral_prod_epsHom_flucDiag_le_graded, RBM.Gauss.integral_norm_flucAvg_pow_le_iter_graded, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_flucAvg_iter_graded, RBM.Gauss.momentDom_flucAvg_iter, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_flucAvg_iter, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_flucAvg_blockAvg_iter, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_flucAvg_Sblk_iter, RBM.Gauss.trace_green_sub_mul_Eblk_stochDom_iter}
  \leanok\uses{lem:fluc-vanish, lem:fluc-count, lem:fluc-avg, lem:condrow}
  Each pivot expands $1=Q_\kappa+P_\kappa$ in the non-pivot slots, kills the all-$P$ term by the
  vanishing lemma and buys one factor $\rho$; after full depth a multi-index with $a$ lone slots
  gains $\rho^a$, and $2\,\#\mathrm{image}\,v\le n+a$ gives $\rho^{n-a}$ from the weights, so every
  stratum contributes $\rho^n$: $\mathbb E|\sum_kt_kZ_k|^{2p}\lesssim_p(\rho B)^{2p}$, i.e. (4.12).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Induction on the number of remaining pivots, the state being one word $\texttt{List}(\mathrm{Bool}
  \times\mathrm{Idx})$ per slot; $\mathbb E_k$ and $\mathbb E_\kappa$ commute for any two rows
  (\texttt{condRow\_condRow\_comm}), which is what lets the innermost $Q$ survive the growing word.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The time net with a modulus holding only with high probability]\label{lem:holder-hp}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.stochDom_of_subset_highProb, RBM.Gauss.netTime, RBM.Gauss.netTime_mem, RBM.Gauss.exists_netTime_close, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_timeIcc_of_holder_hp, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_Icc_of_holder_hp, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_timeIcc_one_of_holder_hp, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_timeIcc_of_holder_dom, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_timeIcc_one_of_holder_dom}
  \leanok\uses{lem:moment-dom, def:2.1-i}
  The $N^{-C}$ net of (5.46) needs its H\"older modulus only eventually in $N$ and on a
  high-probability family $\Xi$, or with a random constant $R\prec1$; the interval may be the
  $N$-dependent $[s_N,t_N]$. T73 and T75 are the special cases $\Xi\equiv\mathrm{univ}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $\mathrm{badSet}\subseteq(\mathrm{badSet}\cap\Xi)\cup\Xi^c$; for the random constant,
  $\{R\le N\}$ is high-probability and there the modulus is deterministic, costing one power of $N$
  in the net exponent.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[$\|X\|\prec1$ from the trace moments]\label{lem:opnorm}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.frobSq, RBM.Gauss.l2_opNorm_sq_le_frobSq, RBM.Gauss.l2_opNorm_pow_two_pow, RBM.Gauss.l2_opNorm_pow_le_frobSq, RBM.Gauss.trace_pow_eq_frobSq, RBM.Gauss.measurable_norm_Xmat, RBM.Gauss.integrable_norm_Xmat_pow, RBM.Gauss.integrable_frobSq_Xmat_pow, RBM.Gauss.TraceMomentBound, RBM.Gauss.integral_frobSq_Xmat_one, RBM.Gauss.traceMomentBound_one, RBM.Gauss.pow_le_add_inv_mul_pow, RBM.Gauss.integral_norm_Xmat_pow_le, RBM.Gauss.momentDom_norm_Xmat, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_norm_Xmat, RBM.Gauss.opNormBound_of_traceMomentBound}
  \leanok\uses{def:gauss-model, lem:moment-dom}
  Given $\mathbb E\operatorname{Tr}X^{2p}\le C_pN$, the operator norm satisfies $\|X\|\prec1$,
  discharging \texttt{OpNormBound}. The step $\|A\|^{2q}\le\operatorname{Tr}A^{2q}$ avoids the
  spectral theorem by iterating the C*-identity at $q=2^m$; the trace bound
  $\mathbb E\operatorname{Tr}X^2=WL\le N$ is unconditional.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $g^{2p}\le M^{2p}+M^{-2r}g^{2(p+r)}$ with $M=N^{\varepsilon/2}$ and $p+r$ a power of two bridges
  the quantifier order of \texttt{MomentDom}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lemma 4.1 along the flow, for the Gaussian model]\label{thm:lemma41-flow}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.stochDom_mono_left, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_trans_indicator_idx, RBM.Gauss.flowDelta, RBM.Gauss.goodEv_subset_goodSet_flow, RBM.Gauss.EntryBoundFlow, RBM.Gauss.DiagBoundFlow, RBM.Gauss.LoopHypFlow, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_Lmax_flow, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_entryControl_flow, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_diag_flow, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_offdiag_flow, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_llMax_sq_flow, RBM.Gauss.lemma41Flow, RBM.Gauss.step1Phi_eq, RBM.Gauss.slow_step1Phi, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_timeIcc_of_holder_slow_hp, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_timeIcc_of_holder_slow_dom}
  \leanok\uses{lem:4.1, lem:holder-hp, def:gauss-model, thm:step1}
  From the time-indexed (4.2)/(4.3) one gets \texttt{Step1.Lemma41Flow} for \emph{every}
  $\Phi\ge0$: the transfer is between two dominations that are already uniform in time, so no
  time net and no slow variation of $\Phi$ is needed. The net bridge with a slowly varying
  $\Phi(N,u)$ is supplied anyway, with the spacing exposed so the hypothesis is dischargeable;
  for Step 1's own $\Phi=(\ell_sW\eta_u)^{-1}$ it reduces to the net spacing being below $1-t_N$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Each step is a statement about the failure event at one $N$ and $\omega$ with an existential over
  the index set, so enlarging that set by the time is harmless; the good event at time $u$ sits
  inside the time-frozen one by monotonicity of the scale.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Gaussian integration by parts: the p.\,50 display, and \texttt{hIBP}]\label{lem:ibp}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.green_sub_smul_one_eq, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_green_Hflow_update, RBM.Gauss.mul_Bmat_mul_apply_of_ne, RBM.Gauss.mul_Bmat_mul_apply_diag, RBM.Gauss.tame_green_apply, RBM.Gauss.integral_coord_mul_green_apply, RBM.Gauss.sum_gvar_Bmat_sandwich_diag, RBM.Gauss.integral_Hflow_mul_green_diag, RBM.Gauss.Tame.comp_rowSplit, RBM.Gauss.rowSplit_update, RBM.Gauss.condRow_coord_mul, RBM.Gauss.Bmat_mul_apply_diag_of_ne, RBM.Gauss.condRow_Hflow_mul_green_diag, RBM.Gauss.condExpDiag_eq_sum_Sblk, RBM.Gauss.ibpRem, RBM.Gauss.ibpRem_eq_add, RBM.Gauss.norm_condExpDiag_sub_le, RBM.Gauss.condExpDiag_stochDom_of_ibpRem, RBM.Gauss.condExpDiag_stochDom_of_pieces}
  \leanok\uses{def:gauss-model, lem:condrow, lem:fluc-avg, lem:4.2}
  $\mathbb E_i(G_{ii}-m)=t\,m\sum_kS_{ik}\,\mathbb E_i[G_{ii}(G_{kk}-m)]$ as an exact identity, the
  whole $O_\prec(\Psi^2)$ isolated in one remainder; hence \texttt{hIBP} at its frozen signature,
  given the local law squared and the (4.9) minor replacement.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  One Gaussian coordinate at a time, the two tags recombining to $S_{ik}$ (the squared off-diagonal
  terms cancel); $\mathbb E_i$ is itself an integral against a frozen row, and \texttt{rowSplit}
  commutes with updating a row-$i$ coordinate, so the same integration by parts applies inside it.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[(2.39) and (6.1) for the Gaussian model: no distributional argument needed]\label{lem:disteq}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.green_smul_mul, RBM.Gauss.Gsig_smul_mul, RBM.Gauss.gloopProd_smul_mul, RBM.Gauss.gloop_smul_mul, RBM.Gauss.zt_eq_sqrt_lemT_mul, RBM.Gauss.sqrt_lemT_ne_zero, RBM.Gauss.green_Hflow_lemT_eq, RBM.Gauss.gloop_Hflow_lemT_eq, RBM.Gauss.gloop_one_Hflow_lemT_eq, RBM.Gauss.gloop_two_Hflow_lemT_eq, RBM.Gauss.gloop_Hflow_ztTilde_eq, RBM.Gauss.transfer_gauss, RBM.Gauss.transfer_gauss_Hband, RBM.Gauss.transferLoop1_gauss, RBM.Gauss.loopScaling_gauss}
  \leanok\uses{def:gauss-model, thm:2.3-2.4, lem:5.1}
  With one fixed $X$ and $H_u=\sqrt u\,X$, the scaling cancels against the shift of the spectral
  parameter: $\sqrt t\,G_t^{(E)}=G(X,z)$ and $L_{t_2}=(t_1/t_2)^{n/2}L_{t_1}$ \emph{pointwise in
  $\omega$}. So \texttt{Transfer}, \texttt{TransferLoop1} and \texttt{LoopScaling} are equalities of
  random variables, not equalities in law.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $z^{(E)}_t=t^{1/2}z$ by (2.37), hence $H_t-z_t=t^{1/2}(X-z)$; loops scale by the factor to the
  power of their length, and $(t_1/t_2)^{n/2}\le1$ finishes the transfer.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The gain is multiplicative: $m$ fluctuations compose into the $m$-fold minor difference]\label{thm:fluc-high}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.AgreeOffRows, RBM.Gauss.FinDepOffRows, RBM.Gauss.qRow_of_finDepOffRows, RBM.Gauss.finDepOffRows_of_minorSet, RBM.Gauss.greenSetMat, RBM.Gauss.greenSetMat_empty_apply, RBM.Gauss.greenSetMat_singleton_apply, RBM.Gauss.flucDiagSet_singleton, RBM.Gauss.minorDiff, RBM.Gauss.qList, RBM.Gauss.applyOps_sub, RBM.Gauss.applyOps_eq_applyOps_minorDiff, RBM.Gauss.norm_applyOps_le_minorDiff, RBM.Gauss.norm_applyOps_flucDiag_le_of_minorDiff, RBM.Gauss.insertRowEquiv, RBM.Gauss.greenSetMat_insert_apply, RBM.Gauss.greenSetDiagCentered_sub_insert, RBM.Gauss.minorDiff_pair_greenSetDiagCentered, RBM.Gauss.norm_minorDiff_pair_greenSetDiagCentered_le, RBM.Gauss.MinorDiffGain, RBM.Gauss.flucGain_of_minorDiffGain, RBM.Gauss.minorDiffGain_env}
  \leanok\uses{thm:fluc-iter, lem:fluc-vanish, lem:condrow, lem:4.2}
  $Q_\kappa$ annihilates anything independent of row $\kappa$ and the $Q$'s commute, so peeling the
  outermost one and passing to $\Delta_\kappa Y=Y_S-Y_{S\cup\{\kappa\}}$ loses nothing: an
  \emph{exact} identity, not an estimate. Hence $\|Q_{\kappa_2}Q_{\kappa_1}Z_k\|\lesssim\Psi^3$ --
  multiplicative, not $2\Psi^2$ -- and \texttt{FlucGain} follows for all $m$ from the size of the
  iterated minor differences.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  At $m=2$, after (4.9) the second difference is a difference of two triple products at levels
  $\emptyset$ and $\{\kappa_2\}$; each factor differs from its partner by $\Psi^2$, the off-diagonals
  are $\Psi$ and the inverse diagonal is $O(1)$, giving $\Psi^3$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[$\mathbb E\operatorname{Tr}X^{2p}\le C_pN$, hence $\|X\|\prec1$ unconditionally]\label{thm:trace-moment}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_matPow_apply, RBM.Gauss.tame_Xmat_pow_apply, RBM.Gauss.Bmat_sandwich_apply_add_of_ne, RBM.Gauss.sum_gvar_Bmat_sandwich_apply, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_const_mul_Xmat_pow_update, RBM.Gauss.integral_Xmat_pow_succ_diag, RBM.Gauss.colSq, RBM.Gauss.colSq_sq_le, RBM.Gauss.pow_two_mul_apply_diag, RBM.Gauss.pow_le_pow_of_logConvex, RBM.Gauss.sqrt_colSq_le_rpow, RBM.Gauss.norm_mul_norm_pow_diag_le, RBM.Gauss.traceConst, RBM.Gauss.integral_colSq_one, RBM.Gauss.integral_colSq_le, RBM.Gauss.traceMomentBound_gauss, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_norm_Xmat_gauss, RBM.Gauss.opNormBound_gauss}
  \leanok\uses{lem:opnorm, lem:ibp, def:gauss-model}
  Gaussian integration by parts gives
  $\mathbb E[(X^{m+1})_{aa}]=\sum_{k+l=m-1}\mathbb E[(\sum_jS_{aj}(X^k)_{jj})(X^l)_{aa}]$; the
  recursion is closed not by Cauchy--Schwarz (which doubles the degree) but by log-convexity of the
  column norms plus weighted AM--GM, giving $C_p=(2p-1)!!$ and discharging \texttt{OpNormBound}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $u_m(i)=(X^{2m})_{ii}$ satisfies $u_m^2\le u_{m-1}u_{m+1}$ and $u_0=1$, so
  $|(X^k)_{ii}|\le u_M(i)^{k/2M}$ and the product of two such is a convex combination -- the degrees
  never double. Induction on $M$ with $\sum_jS_{ij}=1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The $m$-fold minor difference: a Leibniz calculus for $\Delta_\kappa$]\label{thm:minordiff}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.MinorGoodLe, RBM.Gauss.minorGoodLe_of_goodEvent, RBM.Gauss.MinorGoodLe.goodEvent, RBM.Gauss.isUnit_det_Hflow_submatrix_sub, RBM.Gauss.iterDeltaFam_congr, RBM.Gauss.DiffBd.congr, RBM.Gauss.deltaFam, RBM.Gauss.shiftFam, RBM.Gauss.iterDeltaFam, RBM.Gauss.minorDiff_eq_iterDeltaFam, RBM.Gauss.deltaFam_mul, RBM.Gauss.deltaFam_inv_apply, RBM.Gauss.deltaFam_shiftFam, RBM.Gauss.iterDeltaFam_shiftFam, RBM.Gauss.gFam, RBM.Gauss.gInvFam, RBM.Gauss.MinorGood, RBM.Gauss.minorGood_of_half_le, RBM.Gauss.deltaFam_gFam, RBM.Gauss.deltaFam_gInvFam, RBM.Gauss.DiffBd, RBM.Gauss.DiffBd.mul, RBM.Gauss.atomC, RBM.Gauss.diffBd_atom, RBM.Gauss.deltaFam_greenSetDiagCentered, RBM.Gauss.minorDiffC, RBM.Gauss.norm_minorDiff_greenSetDiagCentered_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_minorDiff_triple_le, RBM.Gauss.minorDiff_qRow, RBM.Gauss.norm_minorDiff_flucDiagSet_le, RBM.Gauss.integral_prod_applyOps_minorDiff_le, RBM.Gauss.minorDiffGain_of_pointwise, RBM.Gauss.MinorGood', RBM.Gauss.minorGood'_of_half_le, RBM.Gauss.minorGood'_of_local_law, RBM.Gauss.norm_greenSetDiagCentered_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_flucDiagSet_le, RBM.Gauss.integral_prod_applyOps_minorDiff_le', RBM.Gauss.MinorDiffGainUpTo, RBM.Gauss.MinorDiffGain.upTo, RBM.Gauss.flucGainUpTo_of_minorDiffGainUpTo, RBM.Gauss.minorDiffGainUpTo_of_minorGood, RBM.Gauss.minorDiffGainUpTo_of_minorGood', RBM.Gauss.flucGainUpTo_of_minorDiff, RBM.Gauss.flucGainUpTo_of_minorDiff', RBM.Gauss.MinorGood'.toMinorGoodLe}
  \leanok\uses{thm:fluc-high, lem:4.2, lem:condrow}
  The hypothesis is now \texttt{MinorGoodLe}, the good event carried to levels $|S|\le M$ by
  iterating (4.9) from level $0$, where it \emph{is} \texttt{GoodEvent}.  The all-levels form it
  replaces was false for every $\Psi$, so \texttt{DiffBd} carries a matching budget
  $|S|+|l|\le M$; a difference and a shift each spend one unit, and the atom's invariant
  $B+|T|\le M$ is conserved.  Inside a budget only pointwise equalities survive, which is what
  \texttt{iterDeltaFam\_congr} supplies.

  \emph{Two qualifications.}  The falsity of \texttt{MinorGood} and \texttt{MinorGood'} is an
  argument, not a committed counterexample: at $X=0$ one gets
  $\|G^{(S)}_{aa}-m\|=u/(1-u)$, independent of $N$, which together with $2\Psi\le1$ forces
  $u\le1/3$, while Step 2 lives at $t>s\ge1/2$; and it is not an almost-everywhere exception,
  since $G$ depends continuously on finitely many coordinates.  The witnesses for this were built
  as probes and are \emph{not} in the repository, so what the library records is the positive
  direction only, \texttt{MinorGood'.toMinorGoodLe}, which states that the replacement merely
  weakened the premise.  Second, iterating to $|S|=m$ is the formalization's construction, not the
  paper's: the paper gives only the level-$0$ bounds (4.2)/(4.3) and the one-step identity (4.9),
  and always eliminates the superscript immediately.

  $\Delta_\kappa(YZ)=(\Delta_\kappa Y)Z+Y^{(\kappa)}\Delta_\kappa Z$ and
  $\Delta_\kappa(1/Y)=-(\Delta_\kappa Y)/(YY^{(\kappa)})$, with (4.9) as
  $\Delta_\kappa G_{ab}=G_{a\kappa}G_{\kappa b}G_{\kappa\kappa}^{-1}$: each difference gains one
  $\Psi$, so $\|\Delta_{\kappa_1}\cdots\Delta_{\kappa_m}(G^{(\cdot)}_{kk}-m)\|\le C_m\Psi^{m+1}$ for
  every $m$, on the event where the local law holds at every minor level.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Atoms carry a base level, so the class is closed under the level shift and the induction closes;
  the freshness condition is exactly what the grading needs.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[$\prec$ under the conditional expectation, and \texttt{hIBP} discharged]\label{thm:conddom}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.lintegral_measure_rowSlice, RBM.Gauss.meas_measure_rowSlice_ge, RBM.Gauss.norm_condRow_le_split, RBM.Gauss.norm_condRow_le_const, RBM.Gauss.CondStable, RBM.Gauss.CondStable.of_det, RBM.Gauss.CondStable.of_finDepOffRow, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_condRow_of_envelope, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_condRow_sub_self, RBM.Gauss.measurable_Lmax, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_rpow_neg_one_Lmax, RBM.Gauss.hprod_of_localLaw, RBM.Gauss.hminor_offdiag_of_repl, RBM.Gauss.norm_ibpRem_le_env, RBM.Gauss.norm_condExpDiag_sub_le_offdiag, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_ibpRem_offdiag, RBM.Gauss.condExpDiag_stochDom_of_offdiag, RBM.Gauss.condExpDiag_stochDom_of_localLaw, RBM.Gauss.trace_green_sub_mul_Eblk_stochDom_of_localLaw}
  \leanok\uses{lem:ibp, lem:condrow, lem:envelope, lem:4.2}
  Domination is \emph{not} preserved by $\mathbb E_k$: the exceptional event survives integration.
  With a deterministic envelope of polynomial growth, a control that is not super-polynomially
  small, and $\mathbb E_k[\zeta]\prec\chi$, it is: $\|X\|\prec\zeta\Rightarrow
  \|\mathbb E_k[X]\|\prec\chi$. Hence \texttt{hIBP} at its frozen signature.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $\int P(\text{row slice})\,dP=P(S)$ by Fubini through the row-splitting map, then Markov: the row
  section of the bad event is small outside a small set of $\omega$; the good part is bounded by
  $N^\tau\mathbb E_k[\zeta]$ and the bad part by the envelope.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[(4.12) conditioned on the good event (4.1): the level tower and the cardinality budget]\label{thm:eq412-cond}
