  \lean{RBM.Gauss.badStep, RBM.Gauss.subset_badStep, RBM.Gauss.measurableSet_badStep, RBM.Gauss.measureReal_rowSlice_le_of_notMem_badStep, RBM.Gauss.badTower, RBM.Gauss.badTower_zero, RBM.Gauss.badTower_succ, RBM.Gauss.BadFamily, RBM.Gauss.badFamily_badTower, RBM.Gauss.meas_badStep_le, RBM.Gauss.meas_badTower_le, RBM.Gauss.minorDiff_flucDiagSet_eq, RBM.Gauss.norm_applyOps_le_badFamily, RBM.Gauss.norm_applyOps_minorDiff_flucDiagSet_le_badFamily, RBM.Gauss.condEnv, RBM.Gauss.condCost, RBM.Gauss.one_le_condEnv, RBM.Gauss.condEps, RBM.Gauss.norm_applyOps_minorDiff_flucDiagSet_le_condEnv, RBM.Gauss.integral_prod_applyOps_minorDiff_le_on, RBM.Gauss.badBase, RBM.Gauss.minorGoodLe_of_notMem_badBase, RBM.Gauss.integral_prod_applyOps_minorDiff_le_goodSetFlow, RBM.Gauss.integral_pow_norm_flucDiag_le_of_flucGainUpTo, RBM.Gauss.integral_pow_norm_flucDiag_le_of_minorDiffGainUpTo, RBM.Gauss.FlucGainUpTo', RBM.Gauss.MinorDiffGainUpTo', RBM.Gauss.FlucGainUpTo.budget, RBM.Gauss.MinorDiffGainUpTo.budget, RBM.Gauss.integral_pow_norm_flucDiag_le_of_flucGainUpTo', RBM.Gauss.integral_pow_norm_flucDiag_le_of_minorDiffGainUpTo', RBM.Gauss.minorDiffGainUpTo'_of_le_on, RBM.Gauss.flucGainUpTo'_of_le_on, RBM.Gauss.minorDiffGainUpTo'_goodSetFlow, RBM.Gauss.flucGainUpTo'_goodSetFlow, RBM.Gauss.minorDiffGain_budget_hyps_consistent, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_flucAvg_iter_budget, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_flucAvg_blockAvg_iter_budget, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_flucAvg_Sblk_iter_budget, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_flucRow_condExpDiag_psi_budget, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_flucBlk_condExpDiag_psi_budget, RBM.Gauss.eq45Flow_of_localLaw_gain_budget, RBM.Gauss.eq45Flow_of_goodSetFlow_budget}
  \leanok\uses{thm:minordiff, thm:conddom, thm:fluc-iter, lem:goodsetflow, lem:condstableflow, lem:eq45fix}
  The route from (4.12) to (4.5) as it now runs: the gain of the $m$-fold minor difference is
  used \emph{on an event} rather than at every sample point, and the interfaces that carry it are
  given a second budget, on the cardinality of the index set, without which they are
  unsatisfiable at the size the paper needs.

  \emph{Conditioning.}  The naive shape -- a row-conditional bound
  $\mathbb P_\kappa(\mathrm{Bad})\le\varepsilon$ for \emph{every} $\omega$ -- is itself
  unsatisfiable: freeze the diagonal coordinate of one row $j\ne\kappa$ at a large value and the
  slice over row $\kappa$ is everything, so the conditional probability is $1$ whatever $\kappa$
  does.  Markov does not repair it, because moving those $\omega$ into $\mathrm{Bad}$ changes the
  slices again.  The shape that works is a tower, one level per letter:
  \texttt{badStep}/\texttt{badTower} with the slice condition guarded by
  $\omega\notin\mathrm{Bad}(j+1)$, so that it holds \emph{by construction} and is not a fiat;
  \texttt{badFamily\_badTower} produces a \texttt{BadFamily} unconditionally from any measurable
  set, at the cost \texttt{meas\_badTower\_le} of one factor $(\varepsilon+\#\mathrm{rows})
  /\varepsilon=N^{O(1)}$ per letter, which $\prec$ tolerates at bounded word length
  (paper-deltas \#122: the paper never states a conditional version).  The word is
  then estimated letter by letter, $\mathbb E_\kappa$ by \texttt{norm\_condRow\_le\_split} and
  $Q_\kappa$ by one triangle inequality, with \emph{no indicator multiplied into the integrand};
  the structural step that makes this possible is
  \texttt{minorDiff\_flucDiagSet\_eq}, which exhibits the whole factor as a single word applied to
  a \emph{deterministic} family, so the good event is used pointwise only there.  The resulting
  \texttt{integral\_prod\_applyOps\_minorDiff\_le\_on} and its
  \texttt{goodSetFlow} form have no hypothesis quantified over all $\omega$: the only
  probabilistic input is (4.1).  The price is explicit -- \texttt{condEnv} and \texttt{condCost},
  added to the constant $B$ with the gain $\rho=2\Psi$ undegraded, plus an additive remainder
  $\texttt{condEnv}^{\#\iota}\,\mathbb P(\mathrm{Bad})$.  Measurability of the flow good set is
  handled by passing to \texttt{toMeasurable}, not by assuming it away.

  \emph{Why a budget is needed at all -- a ceiling, not a collapse.}  Taking $\iota=\mathrm{Fin}\,n$
  and all words empty, \texttt{integral\_pow\_norm\_flucDiag\_le\_of\_minorDiffGainUpTo} (and,
  on the live path, the same statement for \texttt{FlucGainUpTo}) shows that the unbudgeted
  interface forces its constant $B$ to dominate $\|Z_k\|_{L^j}$ for \emph{every} $j$, hence
  $\|Z_k\|_\infty$, which a two-point argument bounds below by $64/65$; so $B\asymp\Psi$ cannot be
  arranged and \texttt{minorDiffGainUpTo\_of\_minorGood'} is a true theorem with a false premise.
  \emph{Honestly}: the half that is compiled is ``$B$ dominates every $L^j$ norm'';
  the two-point lower bound and the $L^n\to L^\infty$ passage, which needs continuity of
  $\omega\mapsto Z_k(\omega)$, are \emph{not} in the repository, so the unsatisfiability claim is
  ``old interface $+$ that uncompiled input''.  The remainder is not an artefact of the
  conditioning either: $\texttt{condEnv}\asymp\eta_u^{-1}>B$, so with $\#\iota$ unbounded it
  cannot be absorbed.

  \emph{The budget.}  \texttt{FlucGainUpTo'} and \texttt{MinorDiffGainUpTo'} add
  $\#\iota\le n$ beside the existing word-length budget $M$.  This is free: the interface is used
  exactly once in the whole chain, where $\iota=\mathrm{Fin}\,p\oplus\mathrm{Fin}\,p$ has
  cardinality $2p$, so $M=n=2p$, the same source as \texttt{OpsOkOut.length\_le}; everything
  downstream takes an already-fixed $\iota$ and is unchanged.  The primed conclusions hold only
  for $j\le n$, which is precisely what blocks the derivation above.  With the budget the
  remainder absorbs: for $B_0=2\,\texttt{minorDiffC}\,M\,\Psi+\texttt{condCost}$ and
  $\#\iota\le n$, $\sum q\le nM$, $B_0\le1$, $2\Psi\le1$, $1\le\texttt{condEnv}$,
  $\texttt{condEnv}^{\#\iota}\mathbb P(\mathrm{Bad})\le B_0^{\#\iota}(2\Psi)^{\sum q}$, giving
  $B=2B_0$ with $\rho=2\Psi$ intact.  $\#\iota=0$ genuinely has to be treated apart: the general
  form would read $1+\mathbb P(\mathrm{Bad})\le1$, which is false, while the truth there is
  $\int1=1$.  End to end, \texttt{eq45Flow\_of\_goodSetFlow\_budget} runs from (4.1) to
  \texttt{StepGlue.Eq45Flow} with the gain produced on the spot and the paper's sizes,
  $\Psi_N=2\delta_N$, $\rho=2\Psi_N$, control $\rho B\asymp\Psi^2$.

  \emph{Status of the old interfaces}: \texttt{FlucGainUpTo} and \texttt{MinorDiffGainUpTo} and
  all their consumers are untouched, and \texttt{.budget} ships the stronger to the weaker so no
  existing supply is lost; they are not to be used as hypotheses any more (paper-deltas \#126).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  \texttt{minorDiffGain\_budget\_hyps\_consistent} exhibits, for any energy, time and budget,
  explicit $\Psi>0$, $\varepsilon>0$ and $\mathbb P(\mathrm{Bad})>0$ making
  $2\Psi\le1$, $B_0\le1$ and the smallness condition hold at once, with $B\asymp\Psi$ -- and the
  bad probability is \emph{strictly} positive, so this is not the trivial witness.  In the
  opposite direction, feeding the degenerate script of the ceiling argument to the budgeted
  interface leaves the goal $j\le n$, which is unprovable: the budget is what stops it.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The tower's remainder from (4.1) alone, and the quantifier order (4.12) really needs]\label{lem:eq412-small}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.PolyLo, RBM.Gauss.PolyHi, RBM.Gauss.polyLo_of_one_div_rpow_le, RBM.Gauss.polyHi_of_sq_le, RBM.Gauss.measureReal_compl_le_of_polyLo, RBM.Gauss.hsmall_of_highProb_aux, RBM.Gauss.hsmall_of_highProb, RBM.Gauss.eq45Flow_of_goodSetFlow_highProb, RBM.Gauss.not_forall_mul_le_one, RBM.Gauss.eq45Flow_delta_hyps_consistent}
  \leanok\uses{thm:eq412-cond, lem:goodsetflow, lem:eq45flow}
  The smallness condition left over by the budget,
  $\texttt{condEnv}^n\,\mathbb P(\texttt{badTower})\le B_0^n(2\Psi)^{nM}$, is now a theorem, and
  its only probabilistic input is (4.1) itself: \texttt{eq45Flow\_of\_goodSetFlow\_highProb} runs
  from the good event to \texttt{StepGlue.Eq45Flow} with no such hypothesis in its statement.  No
  new assumption is introduced -- the four regime bounds it needs
  ($\delta_N$ not super-polynomially small, $\eta_{t_N}^{-1}$ polynomially large, at most $N$
  rows, monotonicity of $\eta$ in the time) are all already hypotheses of that theorem.  The
  bookkeeping is done by two one-line predicates, \texttt{PolyLo} and \texttt{PolyHi}, with
  closure under products, powers, sums, inverses and quotients; high probability is used in
  exactly one place, \texttt{measureReal\_compl\_le\_of\_polyLo}, where the complement of a
  high-probability event is eventually dominated by \emph{any} \texttt{PolyLo} function -- that is
  where the ``for every $D$'' is spent.  These two predicates are generic tools and should be sunk
  into the shared definitions if a second consumer appears.

  \emph{A vacuity found and fixed.}  Checked for satisfiability, the earlier end-to-end theorem
  turned out to be vacuously true: its hypotheses $\forall p\,\forall N,\ 8(2p)\delta_N\le1$ and
  the companion bound involving $\texttt{minorDiffC}(2p)=4^{2p}\texttt{atomC}(2p)^3$ are
  incompatible with $\delta_N>0$, since at fixed $N$ the left sides are unbounded in $p$
  (\texttt{not\_forall\_mul\_le\_one}).  The paper's quantifier order is the other one -- $p$
  fixed, $N\to\infty$, $\delta_N\to0$ -- so the four $p$-indexed hypotheses were rewritten as
  ``$\forall p$, eventually in $N$'', which \emph{weakens} them and leaves the conclusion word for
  word unchanged.  \texttt{eq45Flow\_delta\_hyps\_consistent} is the positive witness, with
  $\delta_N=(N+4)^{-28}$ and $K=12$; what it really verifies is that the lower bound on $\delta_N$
  and the fineness upper bound $\delta_N\le\eta^6/(16N^6)$ do not conflict, and they are
  compatible only because $K$ is free: at $K=0$ the purification condition would demand
  $\eta^6\ge16$ with $\eta\le1$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  \texttt{meas\_badTower\_le} lives in $[0,\infty]$; converting it to the reals is free because
  the measure is a probability measure, and the regime bounds then close the estimate by the
  closure lemmas.  The case $\#\iota=0$ needs no separate treatment here: with $M=n=2p$ and $p=0$
  both sides are $1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The decay rate: $1-\|\rho(\xi)\|\asymp|1-\xi|^{1/2}$]\label{lem:rate}
  \lean{RBM.sqrt_norm_one_sub_le_three_mul, RBM.one_sub_norm_rho_le_two_mul_sqrt, RBM.one_sub_norm_rho_asymp, RBM.ellHat_nonneg, RBM.ellHat_mul_one_sub_norm_rho_le, RBM.min_le_ellHat, RBM.norm_rho_pow_le_exp}
  \leanok\uses{lem:Theta-decay-complex, lem:prefactor}
  Two-sided with absolute constants for every $0<\|\xi\|<1$, hence
  $\hat\ell\asymp\min(1/(1-\|\rho\|),L)$ and
  $\|\rho\|^n\le e\cdot e^{-n/(3\hat\ell)}$ for $n\le L$ -- the exponential factor of (2.52) for
  complex $\xi$ by the closed form, with no contour shift and no Poisson summation.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $1-\xi=(1-\rho)^2/(1+\rho+\rho^2)$: $\xi=1$ is a \emph{double} root, which is where the square
  root comes from. The direction problem ($\rho$ near the circle but far from $1$) is excluded by
  $\|\xi\|<1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The deterministic glue between Steps 1--2 and Steps 3--5]\label{lem:stepglue}
  \lean{RBM.StepGlue.stochDom_flowXiLK, RBM.StepGlue.norm_lkErr_one_le, RBM.StepGlue.eventually_R4_le_rpow_quarter, RBM.StepGlue.flow_S_zero, RBM.StepGlue.flow_lkErr_one_le, RBM.StepGlue.flow_S_one, RBM.StepGlue.AprioriDecayAll, RBM.StepGlue.Eq45Flow, RBM.StepGlue.aprioriDecay_pm, RBM.StepGlue.flow_xiLK_two_le, RBM.StepGlue.flow_hs2, RBM.StepGlue.flow_S_two, RBM.StepGlue.flow_S_le_two, RBM.StepGlue.flow_hs1, RBM.StepGlue.flow_sharpLoop_glue, RBM.StepGlue.flow_steps45_glue}
  \leanok\uses{thm:step1, thm:step2, thm:step3, thm:step45}
  $S(m,0)$ for all $m$ and $S(1,l)$ are unconditional, from (2.73)+(2.59) and from the local law
  alone; $S(2,l)$ and Step 4's base cases follow once (2.76) is available for \emph{all four}
  charges. \texttt{flow\_sharpLoop\_glue} and \texttt{flow\_steps45\_glue} feed these into the real
  slots and conclude in the shapes of \texttt{sharpLoop}, \texttt{sharpLmK}, \texttt{sharpDecay}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $(-,+)$ reduces to $(+,-)$ by cyclicity of the trace and $(-,-)$ to $(+,+)$ by conjugation; the
  gain in \texttt{hregS} supplies $(\eta_s/\eta_u)^4\le(W\ell_u\eta_u)^{1/4}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Step 1's continuity event, and the net lift reduced]\label{lem:step1hyp}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.continuousOn_of_sqrt_modulus, RBM.Gauss.norm_green_flow_sub_le_sqrt, RBM.Gauss.abs_llMax_sub_le_sqrt, RBM.Gauss.continuousOn_llMax, RBM.Gauss.highProb_norm_Xmat_le, RBM.Gauss.cont_gauss, RBM.Gauss.eventually_forall_measure_slice_le, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_reindex_of_forall_seq, RBM.Gauss.netTimeIcc, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_of_forall_seq_relaxed, RBM.Gauss.netLift_of_modulus_hp, RBM.Gauss.netLift_of_relaxed, RBM.Gauss.step1Hyp_gauss}
  \leanok\uses{thm:step1, lem:holder-hp, thm:trace-moment, lem:disteq}
  $u\mapsto\|G_u-m\|_{\max}$ is continuous on $[s_N,t_N]$ for \emph{every} sample, since
  $\|G_u-G_{u'}\|\le\eta_t^{-2}(\|X\|+1)|u-u'|^{1/2}$. The net lift is not bypassable: \texttt{StochDom}
  puts the union over the index set inside the probability, so the hypothesis controls one time per
  $N$ while the conclusion needs an uncountable union. Half of that is free
  (\texttt{eventually\_forall\_measure\_slice\_le}); the rest needs the net, in the relaxed form the
  indicator forces.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  A choice of one time per $N$ is a sequence, so the bound holds for every $N$-dependent choice by
  \texttt{by\_contra} plus choice; the indicator $1(\|G_u\|_{\max}\le2)$ has no modulus in $u$, so
  the net points must carry a raised threshold.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The local-law side of \texttt{hIBP}, and $L^{\max}\asymp W^{-1}$ at fixed $t$]\label{lem:condstable}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.stochDom_normSq_green_diag_sub_Lmax, RBM.Gauss.isUnit_det_Hflow_sub, RBM.Gauss.green_Hflow_diag_ne_zero, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_normSq_green_offdiag_Lmax, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_greenDiagCentered_sub_minor_Lmax, RBM.Gauss.Lmax_Hflow_le_inv_W, RBM.Gauss.integrable_Lmax_rowSplit, RBM.Gauss.LmaxRowProxy, RBM.Gauss.condStable_Lmax_of_rowProxy, RBM.Gauss.lmaxRowProxy_inv_W, RBM.Gauss.condStable_Lmax, RBM.Gauss.highProb_goodSet_of_stochDom, RBM.Gauss.condExpDiag_stochDom_of_highProb, RBM.Gauss.trace_green_sub_mul_Eblk_stochDom_of_highProb}
  \leanok\uses{thm:conddom, lem:ibp, lem:4.1, lem:condrow}
  Ward's identity gives $L^{\max}\le\eta_t^{-2}W^{-1}$ on the whole space, and $W^{-1}\le4L^{\max}$
  on the good event: at a \emph{fixed} $t<1$ the control is deterministic up to an $N$-independent
  factor, so $\mathbb E_i[L^{\max}]\prec L^{\max}$ is not a local-law comparison at all. Five of the
  eight hypotheses of \texttt{hIBP}'s final theorem are thereby eliminated.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  The minor identification needs no event: $H_t$ is Hermitian and $\operatorname{Im}z_t\ne0$, so the
  determinant is a unit for every sample. The replacement error is redone from (4.2) with control
  $L^{\max}$, bypassing any $\Psi^2\asymp L^{\max}$ identification.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[(5.127) exactly, and Step 6's integrability]\label{lem:eq527}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.trace_green_mul_Eblk_sub_mE, RBM.Gauss.sum_Sblk_eq_sum_SB_blkCoef, RBM.Gauss.sum_blkCoef_sum_Sblk, RBM.Gauss.eq527_at, RBM.Gauss.eq527_gauss, RBM.Gauss.integrable_sample_lkErr_mul, RBM.Gauss.int2_gauss, RBM.Gauss.norm_quad11_le_integral, RBM.Gauss.norm_quad13_le_integral}
  \leanok\uses{thm:step6, lem:ibp, def:gauss-model}
  (5.127) holds as an \emph{exact} identity, with no error term and no $\prec$: block-averaging
  $G-m=m(-H-tm)G$, one Gaussian integration by parts, and the block collapse
  $\sum_kS_{pk}\mathbb E[G_{pp}G_{kk}]=\sum_bS^{(B)}_{ba}\mathbb E[\langle GE_a\rangle\langle
  GE_b\rangle]$, which is exact because $S$ does not depend on the offsets; $\sum_bS^{(B)}_{ba}=1$
  then cancels the cubic terms.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Products of diagonal entries collapse into products of block averages with no approximation.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Charge reductions: the gap is one charge, not four]\label{lem:chargereduce}
  \lean{RBM.ChargeReduce.conj_SB_apply, RBM.ChargeReduce.conj_Theta_apply, RBM.ChargeReduce.ppLoop, RBM.ChargeReduce.AprioriDecayPP, RBM.ChargeReduce.lkErr_two_flip, RBM.ChargeReduce.lkErr_two_const, RBM.ChargeReduce.aprioriDecayAll_of_pp}
  \leanok\uses{lem:stepglue, thm:step2, lem:Theta-sym}
  $(-,+)$ reduces to $(+,-)$ by cyclicity of the trace and $(-,-)$ to $(+,+)$ by conjugation, at the
  same spectral parameter -- the charge is already encoded as $z\mapsto\bar z$. So
  \texttt{AprioriDecayAll} follows from the single statement \texttt{AprioriDecayPP}, at the cost of
  only $|E|\le2$, $0\le s_N$, $t_N<1$. The $K$ side already covers all four charges.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $\overline{(\Theta_\xi)_{ab}}=(\Theta_{\bar\xi})_{ab}$ since $S^{(B)}$ is real; on the $K$ side
  $\xi=u\,m(\sigma_1)m(\sigma_2)$ with $u$ real.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[(4.5) uniformly in time, without a net]\label{lem:eq45flow}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.lkErr_one_eq_norm_trace, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_Lmax_timeIcc, RBM.Gauss.IBPFlow, RBM.Gauss.FlucRowFlow, RBM.Gauss.FlucBlkFlow, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_lkErr_one_Lmax, RBM.Gauss.eq45Flow_of_inputs, RBM.Gauss.eq45Flow_gauss}
  \leanok\uses{lem:4.1, lem:fluc-avg, thm:lemma41-flow, lem:stepglue}
  Like \texttt{Lemma41Flow} and unlike \texttt{NetLift}, this is a transfer between dominations
  already uniform in time, so carrying $u$ inside the index set suffices: no net, no continuity in
  $u$. The $1$-loop $L-K$ \emph{equals} $\|\langle(G_u-m)E_a\rangle\|$ for both charges, the two
  differing by a conjugation invisible to the norm.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  The deterministic bound behind (4.5) is pointwise in $(N,\omega,u)$, so its \texttt{of\_det} step
  is redone with the enlarged index set.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Lemma 5.9: the wrapper from the deterministic core to $\prec$]\label{lem:decaybridge}
  \lean{RBM.DecayBridge.farInd_mul_le_of_loopDecay, RBM.DecayBridge.LKDecayEvent, RBM.DecayBridge.lkDecay_of_highProb, RBM.DecayBridge.mem_lkDecayEvent_of_loopDecay, RBM.DecayBridge.loopDecay_lk_of_event}
  \leanok\uses{lem:5.10, lem:5.14, lem:4.1}
  The two encodings of Definition 5.8 agree, and T59's deterministic Lemma 5.9 is \emph{already}
  about the object \texttt{LKDecay} names -- \texttt{X.Lval} and \texttt{B.Kval} unfold to
  \texttt{gloop} and \texttt{Kgen} by \texttt{rfl}. So a high-probability wrapper suffices; no
  statement on either side needs reshaping.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  \texttt{LKDecayEvent} isolates the pathwise content; what remains is two explicit inequalities on
  the radius and the error, plus the Lemma 4.1 event uniformly in $u$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[First-moment reverse bridge, and Step 6's quadratic inputs]\label{lem:firstmoment}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.oneLoopData, RBM.Gauss.lkErr_le_det, RBM.Gauss.lkErr_le_rpow, RBM.Gauss.lkErr_loopData_le_rpow, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_lkErr_mul, RBM.Gauss.unifDetDom_integral_lkErr_mul, RBM.Gauss.quad11_unifDetDom, RBM.Gauss.quad13_unifDetDom, RBM.Gauss.integrable_sample_lkErr_mul_real, RBM.Gauss.quad11_unifDetDom_gauss, RBM.Gauss.quad13_unifDetDom_gauss}
  \leanok\uses{lem:envelope, thm:step6, lem:eq527}
  $|Y|\prec\Phi$ with a deterministic envelope of polynomial growth gives
  $\int|Y|\prec\Phi$ -- the even-moment bridge never yields the first moment. Hence Step 6's
  \texttt{hq11} and \texttt{hq13} from \texttt{sharpLmK} at $n=1,3$, for an arbitrary sample.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  The split runs on \texttt{toMeasurable} of the exceptional set, whose measure is exactly the outer
  measure Definition 2.1(i) bounds, so no measurability of $Y$ is needed.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The (4.5) flow inputs: a net with a random, time-dependent control]\label{lem:eq45inputs}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.UnifDomIcc, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_timeIcc_of_unifDom, RBM.Gauss.goodSetFlow, RBM.Gauss.flowNetEvent, RBM.Gauss.highProb_flowNetEvent, RBM.Gauss.inv_W_le_Lmax_flow, RBM.Gauss.rpow_neg_le_Lmax_flow, RBM.Gauss.Lre_flow_le_add, RBM.Gauss.Lmax_flow_le_add, RBM.Gauss.Lmax_flow_slow_of_net, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_timeIcc_Lmax_of_unifDom, RBM.Gauss.ibpFlow_of_unifDom, RBM.Gauss.flucRowFlow_of_unifDom, RBM.Gauss.flucBlkFlow_of_unifDom, RBM.Gauss.eq45Flow_of_unifDom}
  \leanok\uses{lem:eq45flow, lem:holder-hp, lem:condstable, thm:trace-moment}
  Unlike the \emph{transfer} of \texttt{Lemma41Flow}, \emph{producing} a domination over
  $[s_N,t_N]$ puts an uncountable $\exists u$ inside $P$, and every fixed-time producer factors
  through a step needing a finite index. So the net is unavoidable; since the control $L^{\max}_u$
  is random and time-dependent, the existing net lemmas do not apply and the engine is built here.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Slow variation of $L^{\max}$ comes from the modulus
  $L^{\max}_v\le L^{\max}_u+(\eta_u^{-1}+\eta_v^{-1})\|G_u-G_v\|$, not from
  $L^{\max}\asymp W^{-1}$, which degrades as $t_N\to1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The constant charge via Lemma 5.11 at $n=2$]\label{thm:step2pp}
  \lean{RBM.Step2PP.scale_last_le, RBM.Step2PP.xiLK_two_improve_of, RBM.Step2PP.xiLK_two_improve, RBM.Step2PP.stochDom_flowXiL, RBM.Step2PP.flow_xiL_apriori_le, RBM.Step2PP.flow_xiLK_one_le, RBM.Step2PP.xiLK_two_init, RBM.Step2PP.BootPP, RBM.Step2PP.xiLK_two_le, RBM.Step2PP.flow_S_two_of, RBM.Step2PP.flow_S_le_two_of, RBM.Step2PP.flow_hs2_of, RBM.Step2PP.flowR_le_ratio, RBM.Step2PP.harith_flowAs, RBM.Step2PP.flow_sharpLoop_glue_of, RBM.Step2PP.flow_steps45_glue_of, RBM.Step2PP.flow_sharpLoop_glue_flowAs, RBM.Step2PP.flow_steps45_glue_flowAs}
  \leanok\uses{lem:chargereduce, lem:stepglue, lem:5.14, thm:step3, thm:step45}
  (2.76) is not changed: $(+,+)$ satisfies (5.82), so Lemma 5.11 at $n=2$ applies and case 1 of
  (7.16) contracts the kernel directly. The bootstrap runs \emph{pathwise} inside a high-probability
  event, since $u\mapsto\Xi^{(L-K)}_{u,2}(\omega)$ is a finite max of continuous functions for every
  $\omega$. Its output $\Xi_{u,2}\prec(W\ell_s\eta_s)^{1/2}$ is weaker than
  \texttt{AprioriDecayPP} but is all the consumers need.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  (5.83) at $n=2$ has both quadratic factors equal to the quantity being estimated; the a priori
  inputs come from (2.73), (2.75) and (2.68) at $s$; the second pass needs no second bootstrap.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lemma 5.1 at a raised threshold, and \texttt{Step1.Hyp} complete]\label{thm:step1lift}
  \lean{RBM.StochDom.continuity_recursion_thr, RBM.Sample.gmaxEventThr, RBM.Sample.gmaxEvent_eq_thr, RBM.Sample.gmaxEventThr_mono, RBM.lemma_5_1_thr, RBM.lemma_5_1'_thr, RBM.Gauss.norm_Gsig_sub_le_green_sub, RBM.Gauss.norm_gchain_sub_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_gloop_sub_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_Lval_sub_le_sqrt, RBM.Gauss.aprioriRhs_eq, RBM.Gauss.pow_one_sub_le_two_mul, RBM.Gauss.aprioriRhs_le_two_mul, RBM.Gauss.rpow_neg_le_aprioriRhs, RBM.Gauss.eq58_seq_thr, RBM.Gauss.eventually_master_small, RBM.Gauss.netLift_gauss, RBM.Gauss.rpow_neg_one_le_one_sub_of_scale_ge, RBM.Gauss.step1Hyp_gauss_of_regime, RBM.Gauss.step1Hyp_gauss_of_scale}
  \leanok\uses{lem:5.1, lem:step1hyp, thm:step1, thm:trace-moment}
  The threshold $2$ is a proof constant of \S6, not mathematics: rescaling $Y_n\mapsto\lambda^nY_n$
  leaves (6.4) and (6.13) invariant and turns $Y_1\le C_0$ into $Y_1\le2$, so
  \texttt{lemma\_5\_1\_thr} holds at any threshold with no part of \S6 reproved. With it,
  \texttt{lift} closes and \texttt{Step1.Hyp} is complete under exactly the hypotheses
  \texttt{Step1.step1} already takes.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $(\ell_u/\ell_s)^{n-1}(W\ell_u\eta_u)^{-n+1}=(\ell_sW\eta_u)^{-n+1}$: the right side of (2.73)
  depends on the time only through $\eta_u$, so slow variation collapses to Bernoulli.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[(4.4) uniformly in time]\label{lem:goodsetflow}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.abs_norm_green_flow_entry_sub_le, RBM.Gauss.LocalLawUnifIcc, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_timeIcc_localLaw, RBM.Gauss.highProb_goodSetFlow_of_localLaw}
  \leanok\uses{lem:eq45inputs, lem:4.1, thm:trace-moment, lem:condstable}
  The Lemma 4.1 good event holds at \emph{every} $u\in[s_N,t_N]$ with high probability, given the
  weak local law and a polynomial margin. The control of the local law is deterministic and
  time-independent, so the net's slow-variation condition is vacuous and the $t_N\to1$ degradation
  of $L^{\max}\asymp W^{-1}$ never arises.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  A deterministic H\"older-$1/2$ modulus for one entry, the net engine at $\gamma=1/2$ on the event
  $\{\|X\|\le N\}$, then Markov and the margin.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Definition 5.4's $E\otimes E$ is a genuine loop, and its (5.77) bound]\label{lem:eebridge}
  \lean{RBM.Band.toDims, RBM.Band.toDims_Idx, RBM.EEBridge.Gsig_mul_conjTranspose_prodList_mul, RBM.EEBridge.rflip, RBM.EEBridge.glueIdx, RBM.EEBridge.glueIdx_wf, RBM.EEBridge.glueIdx_length, RBM.EEBridge.mem_glueIdx, RBM.EEBridge.anchor_mem_glueIdx, RBM.EEBridge.glueLoop_loopCut, RBM.EEBridge.glueJ, RBM.EEBridge.glueJ_of_mem_Icc, RBM.EEBridge.eeTens_eq_eTens, RBM.EEBridge.norm_eeTens_le, RBM.EEBridge.leftArg, RBM.EEBridge.rightArg, RBM.EEBridge.eeArg, RBM.EEBridge.eeBand, RBM.EEBridge.eeField, RBM.EEBridge.norm_eeField_le}
  \leanok\uses{lem:decaybridge, lem:5.10, lem:chargereduce, thm:discharge-bdg}
  $G_\sigma M^\dagger$-conjugation reverses a chain and flips every charge, so the conjugated second
  factor of $E\otimes E$ \emph{is} the $\bar\sigma$-loop read backwards -- which justifies rather
  than merely adopts T74's convention. All three properties \texttt{Decay.norm\_eTens\_le} assumes
  of its gluing are proved for the concrete one, giving (5.77)'s fourth line pathwise.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  \texttt{glueIdx} is well-formed, of length $2n+2$, and contains both $b$ and the anchor; the
  clamped $k$-index is inert on $1\le k\le n$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The fixed-time fluctuation inputs of (4.5)]\label{lem:eq45fix}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.UnifDomIcc.trans, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_of_moment, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_const_Lmax, RBM.Gauss.flucGain_one_env, RBM.Gauss.FlucGain.mono_B, RBM.Gauss.flucGain_one_env_flow, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_flucAvg_blockAvg_env, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_flucRow_condExpDiag, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_flucBlk_condExpDiag, RBM.Gauss.flucRowFlow_of_gain, RBM.Gauss.flucBlkFlow_of_gain}
  \leanok\uses{lem:eq45inputs, thm:fluc-iter, lem:goodsetflow, lem:condstable}
  Markov needs no cardinality bound here: \texttt{UnifDomIcc} bounds the failure probability per
  index \emph{and} per time, the union over $u$ being taken only by the net. The
  $\Psi^2\to L^{\max}$ bridge is free on the flow good event, since only the lower half
  $W^{-1}/4\le L^{\max}_u$ is used and it costs no $\eta^{-1}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  The iterated route's moment bound is a per-$(N,u)$ deterministic inequality whose only eventual
  part never mentions the time.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lemma 5.9 quantitatively: \texttt{LKDecay} unconditional modulo the flow good event]\label{thm:lkdecayquant}
  \lean{RBM.LKDecayQuant.radius_le, RBM.LKDecayQuant.two_div_one_sub_exp_le, RBM.LKDecayQuant.cor35Const_le_bound, RBM.LKDecayQuant.cKexp, RBM.LKDecayQuant.cKbound, RBM.LKDecayQuant.cKdecay_le_bound, RBM.LKDecayQuant.term1_le, RBM.LKDecayQuant.term2_le, RBM.LKDecayQuant.loopDecay_of_half_lt, RBM.LKDecayQuant.FlowGoodSet, RBM.LKDecayQuant.FlowInputs, RBM.LKDecayQuant.highProb_loopDecay_pair, RBM.LKDecayQuant.lkDecay_of_flowInputs, RBM.LKDecayQuant.FlowGoodEv, RBM.LKDecayQuant.FlowLDE, RBM.LKDecayQuant.LDEFlowDom, RBM.LKDecayQuant.FlowDec, RBM.LKDecayQuant.flowNum_choice, RBM.LKDecayQuant.prefactor_le, RBM.LKDecayQuant.highProb_flowDec_of_aprioriDecay, RBM.LKDecayQuant.lkDecay_of_inputs, RBM.LKDecayQuant.LDecayEvent, RBM.LKDecayQuant.LDecay, RBM.LKDecayQuant.lDecay_of_highProb, RBM.LKDecayQuant.lDecay_of_flowInputs, RBM.LKDecayQuant.lemma514_flow_of_flowInputs}
  \leanok\uses{lem:decaybridge, lem:5.10, lem:goodsetflow, lem:5.14}
  Radius $\ell_uN^{\tau/2}$ and the two error halves close explicitly. The decay exponent is bounded
  below \emph{uniformly in $u$} by a two-regime split that is favourable on both sides: where
  $\hat\ell=L$ the statement is vacuous because the target radius already exceeds the ring, and
  otherwise $\hat\ell\sqrt{1-u}=1$ exactly. The $|L|$ half of (5.75) comes out of the same proof as
  a separate predicate.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  A polynomial majorant for \texttt{cKdecay}, via $2/(1-e^{-\lambda})\le4/\lambda$, is the analytic
  input the repository lacked.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[A modulus in the time for the conditional diagonal]\label{lem:condexpmod}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.Xentry_rowSplit_of_row, RBM.Gauss.sum_normSq_col_le, RBM.Gauss.sum_row_ite_le, RBM.Gauss.rowFrobSq, RBM.Gauss.rowFrobSq_rowSplit, RBM.Gauss.rowFrobSq_le, RBM.Gauss.integrable_rowFrobSq, RBM.Gauss.integral_rowFrobSq_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_Xmat_rowSplit_le, RBM.Gauss.integral_norm_Xmat_rowSplit_le, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_condRow_norm_Xmat, RBM.Gauss.norm_condExpDiag_flow_sub_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_condExpDiag_flow_sub_le_rpow}
  \leanok\uses{lem:condrow, thm:trace-moment, lem:eq45inputs, thm:conddom}
  $\mathbb E_k$ resamples one row only, so $\|X\|$ under the row integral is dominated
  \emph{pointwise} by a polynomial in $\|X(\omega)\|$: hence $\mathbb E_k[\|X\|]\prec1$ from the
  operator-norm bound alone, and the modulus holds with that random constant. The sharp
  $\sum_i|M_{ik}|^2\le\|M\|^2$ is essential -- the crude Frobenius bound would cost a factor $N$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Split the resampled row from the original, bound the original half pointwise and the resampled
  half in expectation by $\sum_jS_{kj}+\sum_iS_{ik}=2$; remove the square root by
  $x\le(1+x^2)/2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Loop observables are $C^2$ in the matrix, with deterministic bounds]\label{lem:loopc2}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.BddC2C, RBM.Gauss.BddC2C.bddC2, RBM.Gauss.BddC2.toBddC2C, RBM.Gauss.fderiv2_clm_apply_apply, RBM.Gauss.bddC2C_clm_apply, RBM.Gauss.bddC2C_clm_comp, RBM.Gauss.bddC2C_add, RBM.Gauss.bddC2C_sum, RBM.Gauss.bddC2_finset_sum, RBM.Gauss.bddC2C_matrix_mul, RBM.Gauss.bddC2C_resH, RBM.Gauss.traceCLM, RBM.Gauss.bddC2C_Gsig_mul_Eblk, RBM.Gauss.bddC2C_gloopProdFun, RBM.Gauss.bddC2C_loopObs, RBM.Gauss.le_two_mul_one_add_inv_cube, RBM.Gauss.bddC2_loopObs, RBM.Gauss.testFun_loopObs_of_im_le, RBM.Gauss.testFun_momentFun_loopObs, RBM.Gauss.ukerObs, RBM.Gauss.bddC2_ukerObs, RBM.Gauss.testFun_ukerObs, RBM.Gauss.testFun_momentFun_ukerObs}
  \leanok\uses{thm:moment-gronwall, thm:generator, thm:moment-hierarchy}
  The missing half was the Leibniz step: a genuinely Fr\'echet second-order product rule closes
  \texttt{BddC2} under products, so a \texttt{foldr} of $n$ factors obeys $(B^n,nB^n,n^2B^n)$ with
  no slack and no bound on $n$. Hence \texttt{TestFun} for $L_{\sigma,a}$, for $|L|^{2p}$ and for
  $(U\circ(L-K))_a$, the last because (5.17)'s coefficients do not depend on $H$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  The resolvent's Fr\'echet $C^2$ bounds $\eta^{-1},\eta^{-2},2\eta^{-3}$ were already available;
  $B=2(1+\eta^{-1})^3$ dominates all of them at once so the induction closes exactly.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The motion of $z_u$ produces exactly the $-m$ subtraction]\label{thm:loopito}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_green_path, RBM.Gauss.Gsig_zt, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_ztSig, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_Gsig_zt, RBM.Gauss.cutGlue_cons_one, RBM.Gauss.cutGlue_cons_succ, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_gloopProd_zt, RBM.Gauss.zMotion, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_gloop_zt, RBM.Gauss.eGterm_sub_eGterm, RBM.Gauss.norm_zMotion_le, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_gloop_zt_eGterm, RBM.Gauss.integrable_zMotion_Hflow, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_integral_gloop_zt, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_comp_diag, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_integral_gloop_hierarchy_movingZ, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_sample_ELval_hierarchy, RBM.Gauss.contDiffAt_green_comp, RBM.Gauss.contDiffAt_Gsig_flow, RBM.Gauss.contDiffAt_gloopProd_flow, RBM.Gauss.contDiffAt_gloop_flow, RBM.Gauss.generator_add_zMotion, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_sub_prim, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_sub_prim_of_isPrimitive}
  \leanok\uses{thm:moment-hierarchy, lem:loopc2, thm:generator, lem:ibp}
  $\partial_uL_{\sigma,a}(M,z_u)=\tilde E[G-m]-\tilde E[G]$: the extra term from the moving spectral
  parameter is \emph{precisely} (2.47)'s $-m(\sigma_k)$, so $\tilde G$ is a theorem rather than a
  convention, and the cut-and-glue obligation is only ever needed in its frozen form. The joint
  derivative goes through \texttt{ContDiff.comp}, avoiding the ``continuous partials'' lemma
  Mathlib lacks.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $\partial_z(H-z)^{-1}=G^2$ and $\dot z_u=-m$ double the $k$-th slot; $\langle E_a\rangle=1$,
  $\sum_aS^{(B)}_{ab}=1$ and $W\sum_bE_b=1$ turn that into Definition 2.10(1)'s sum.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Absorbing an additive remainder into $\prec$, and \texttt{EE\_le}]\label{lem:absorb}
  \lean{RBM.le_rpow_mul_of_le_add_rpow_neg, RBM.StochDom.of_add_le, RBM.StochDom.of_highProb_add_rpow_neg, RBM.HighProb.of_eventually_univ, RBM.EEBridge.eeFacts, RBM.EEBridge.rpow_neg_le_eeControl, RBM.EEBridge.eeDecayEvent, RBM.EEBridge.xiLowEvent, RBM.EEBridge.norm_eeField_le_param, RBM.EEBridge.stochDom_norm_eeField}
  \leanok\uses{lem:eebridge, def:2.1-i, lem:5.10}
  A pathwise bound $N^{\tau}\Phi+\varepsilon_N$ with $\varepsilon_N$ super-polynomially small and
  $\Phi$ bounded below by a power gives $\prec\Phi$ -- the lower bound being an \emph{event}, as a
  random control requires. Hence \texttt{Lemma510.EE\_le} for the concrete $E\otimes E$, modulo a
  high-probability lower bound on $\Xi^{(L)}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Split the threshold as $\tau/2$ and absorb the remainder against the lower bound; the
  deterministic half of that bound follows from $\ell_u\le L$, $\eta_u\le1$, $WL\le N$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Moment Duhamel: the drift field pins down $F$, and four of its six fields are produced]\label{thm:momentduhamel}
  \lean{RBM.le_two_mul_add_sqrt_of_sq_le, RBM.intervalIntegrable_prefix, RBM.integral_le_integral_right, RBM.integral_sqrt_mul_le, RBM.sqrt_le_of_isLUB, RBM.sqrt_le_of_integral_le, RBM.hasDerivAt_edgeKer, RBM.hasDerivAt_Uker_apply, RBM.MomentDuhamel.lkFun, RBM.MomentDuhamel.lkFun_H, RBM.MomentDuhamel.genLK, RBM.MomentDuhamel.Hyp, RBM.MomentDuhamel.Hyp.F_unique, RBM.MomentDuhamel.Hyp.F_unique_flow, RBM.MomentDuhamel.eeFun, RBM.MomentDuhamel.eeFun_H, RBM.MomentDuhamel.EEpath_eq_eeField, RBM.MomentDuhamel.Hyp.Fpath, RBM.MomentDuhamel.EEpath, RBM.MomentDuhamel.momNorm, RBM.MomentDuhamel.momNorm_pow, RBM.MomentDuhamel.momentDom_of_momNorm_le, RBM.MomentDuhamel.stochDom_of_momentDuhamel, RBM.MomentDuhamel.momNorm_le_of_integral_le, RBM.MomentDuhamel.drift_driftF, RBM.MomentDuhamel.MomentIneq, RBM.MomentDuhamel.MomentIneqQ, RBM.MomentDuhamel.LkIntegrable, RBM.MomentDuhamel.integrable_gauss, RBM.MomentDuhamel.hypOfMoments, RBM.MomentDuhamel.hypOfMoments_F, RBM.MomentDuhamel.gaussHypOfMoments, RBM.MomentDuhamel.F_eq_driftF_of_window, RBM.MomentDuhamel.momentIneq_body_self, RBM.MomentDuhamel.momentIneqQ_body_self, RBM.MomentDuhamel.momentIneq_of_diffIneq, RBM.MomentDuhamel.memLp_ofReal_of_integrable_rpow, RBM.MomentDuhamel.integral_pow_sub_one_mul_le, RBM.MomentDuhamel.integral_pow_sub_two_mul_le, RBM.MomentDuhamel.holder_first_sharp, RBM.MomentDuhamel.holder_second_sharp, RBM.MomentDuhamel.momNorm_eq_rpow, RBM.MomentDuhamel.rpow_inv_le_of_deriv_le, RBM.MomentDuhamel.diffIneq_of_deriv_le, RBM.MomentDuhamel.cMDval, RBM.MomentDuhamel.cMDval_of_one_le, RBM.MomentDuhamel.momentIneq_of_derivBound, RBM.MomentDuhamel.momentIneqQ_of_diffIneq, RBM.MomentDuhamel.momentIneqQ_of_derivBound}
  \leanok\uses{thm:discharge-bdg, thm:moment-gronwall, thm:loopito, lem:5.10}
  The structure carries the pointwise identity
  $(\partial_u+\mathcal L)(L-K)=\Theta\circ(L-K)+F$ as a field, and \texttt{F\_unique} shows it
  determines $F$ outright -- so the fiat obstruction closes at the level of definitions, not by a
  domination hypothesis. No martingale field. The closing is by integration and a supremum, not by
  Gr\"onwall, which would lose a power of $\eta$.

  \emph{Four of the six fields are now produced, two are not.}  \texttt{hypOfMoments} and its
  Gaussian specialization build a \texttt{Hyp} in which $F$ is \emph{not} a degree of freedom:
  it is \texttt{DriftDef.driftF} by \texttt{rfl}, and \texttt{F\_eq\_driftF\_of\_window} shows
  \emph{any} \texttt{Hyp} on $[0,1)$ has the same $F$.  \texttt{drift} is free once the drift is
  pinned, with no $0<u$ seam -- the derivative there is of the \emph{frozen} matrix, so $\sqrt u$
  never appears; integrability is free for the Gaussian model.  The remaining two,
  \texttt{momentDuhamel} and \texttt{momentDuhamelQ}, are reduced to a single pointwise
  derivative inequality: \texttt{momentIneq\_of\_diffIneq} turns \texttt{hdu} into the field, the
  two H\"older steps and the division by $p\psi^{p-1}$ are theorems
  (\texttt{diffIneq\_of\_deriv\_le}; $\psi=0$ is handled by running the fundamental theorem of
  calculus on $\varphi+\varepsilon$ and letting $\varepsilon\downarrow0$, which is elementary and
  needs no dominated convergence), and \texttt{momentIneq\_of\_derivBound} assembles them.

  \emph{What is still open, and one constant that had to change.}  The remaining input is the
  time-dependent admissibility of $\Psi(u,M)=|(U_{u,v}\circ(L-K)(u,M))_a|^{2p}$ in the sense of
  \ref{lem:momentduhamelgauss}; its one genuine gap is a derivative bound of the loop in the
  \emph{spectral} variable $z$, which the library does not have (what it has is an
  $M$-derivative bound uniform in $z$, a different statement).  The generator forces
  $c_{\mathrm{MD}}(p)=2p-1$, and \texttt{holder\_first\_sharp} /
  \texttt{holder\_second\_sharp} show it cannot be lowered; but the field is quantified over all
  $p:\mathbb N$ and $2p-1$ is negative at $p=0$, so the constant carried is
  $\texttt{cMDval}\,p=\max(0,2p-1)$, equal to $2p-1$ exactly where the inequalities speak.
  The second H\"older step must treat $p=1$ separately: its conjugate exponent $p/(p-1)$ is
  undefined there.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $m^2\le A+2Fm\Rightarrow m\le2F+\sqrt A$; the integration step is the only place the supremum
  enters, which is what makes the right-hand side independent of $u$.
\end{proof}

RBM.Gauss.continuousOn_momNorm_lkT_gauss, RBM.Gauss.continuousOn_momNorm_driftF_gauss, RBM.Gauss.continuousOn_momNorm_eeFun_gauss, RBM.Gauss.intervalIntegrable_phi'_gauss, RBM.Gauss.intervalIntegrable_momNorm_driftF_gauss, RBM.Gauss.intervalIntegrable_momNorm_eeFun_gauss, RBM.Gauss.intervalIntegrable_psi_mul_driftF_gauss, RBM.Gauss.continuousOn_Kprim, RBM.Gauss.exists_bdd_Kval_Kprim, RBM.Gauss.momentIneq_of_derivBound_gauss, RBM.Gauss.uker_driftF_eq_at_zero, RBM.Gauss.sideConditions_gauss_window_zero}
  \leanok\uses{thm:generator, thm:momentduhamel, lem:loopc2}
  $\frac{d}{du}\mathbb E[\Psi(u,H_u)]=\mathbb E[\partial_1\Psi]
  +\tfrac12\sum_{ij}S_{ij}\mathbb E[\partial_{ij}\partial_{ji}\Psi]$ for a test function that
  depends on the time as well as the matrix; the generator identity of T71 is the case
  $\Psi(v,\cdot)=\Phi$.  The admissible class is localised to a time window, which is forced
  rather than cosmetic: every bound on the real $\Psi$ is a power of
  $(\operatorname{Im}z_u)^{-1}=((1-u)\operatorname{Im}m_E)^{-1}$.

  The class \texttt{TestFunT} as first written is too strong in two independent ways, and both
  weakenings are \emph{proved} to be strict rather than assumed to be harmless.  First, in the
  matrix slot: \texttt{TestFunT'} quantifies over Hermitian $M$ only, and its identity is again a
  corollary of the old one through the regularization (\texttt{TestFunT'.herm}), with the usable
  shape being \texttt{hasDerivAt\_integral\_Psi\_pairs\_of\_herm}, which asks only for
  \texttt{ContDiffAt} at Hermitian points plus the old class for $\Psi\circ\mathrm{herm}$ -- and
  both are already available for the loop, since $\mathrm{herm}$ of the raw loop \emph{is}
  \texttt{ukerObs} by definition.  Second, in the time slot: reading the proof line by line shows
  joint $C^2$ is used exactly twice, for joint \emph{differentiability} and for continuity of
  $\partial_1\Psi$ in $M$, so \texttt{TestFunT}$_1$ replaces it by slice $C^2$ plus joint $C^1$ plus
  that continuity, with the four bounds unchanged and the conclusion word for word the same.  The
  enlargement is genuine: $(u,\tfrac12)\mapsto(u-\tfrac12)|u-\tfrac12|$ lies in the new class and
  \texttt{not\_testFunT\_kinkT} proves it does not lie in the old one.  The consequence for the
  flow is concrete -- the \emph{second} time derivative of \texttt{edgeKer} and \texttt{Kval} is
  not needed; the existing first-order facts are already of the right order, and what is short is
  joint regularity.

  \emph{Not closed}: the field \texttt{bddT}, a uniform bound on $\partial_u\Psi$ over the window,
  still needs a derivative bound of the loop in $z$.  What the library has (from the ball-form
  estimates) is an $M$-derivative bound uniform in $z$, which is not the same statement.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  The joint derivative splits as $a\,\partial_1\Psi+\partial_M\Psi[B]$ -- the only place the two
  slots interact.  Joint $C^2$ of the resolvent along the path needs no second-order bounded
  class: $\mathrm{Ring.inverse}$ is $C^\infty$ at units and the inner map is jointly smooth.
  For the weakened classes the original proof is replayed verbatim: had joint $C^2$ been used
  anywhere else, the replay would simply not compile.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The right-hand side of the moment Duhamel inequality]\label{lem:momentduhamelrhs}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.momNorm_mono, RBM.Gauss.momNorm_le_of_le_weighted_sum, RBM.Gauss.sum_prod_norm_edgeKer_le, RBM.Gauss.momNorm_Uker_apply_le, RBM.Gauss.intervalIntegral_le_of_le_const, RBM.Gauss.MomNormDom, RBM.Gauss.momNormDom_of_momentDom, RBM.Gauss.momNormDom_of_stochDom, RBM.Gauss.hFmom_of_momNormDom, RBM.Gauss.hinit_of_momNormDom, RBM.Gauss.hrhs_of_moment_inputs}
  \leanok\uses{thm:momentduhamel, lem:envelope, def:5.2}
  The three terms of the moment Duhamel right-hand side, bounded without rebuilding the
  $\prec$-machinery of \S5.3: in moment form the kernel, the tensor and the time integral
  separate.  The kernel is deterministic and leaves the norm by Minkowski (Jensen on $x^q$,
  since the norm here is a raw $(\int|Y|^q)^{1/q}$); what is left is a $\|\cdot\|_{2p}$ bound on
  the tensor alone, which is the reverse bridge of the envelope lemma; and the time integral is
  pointwise in $u$, so no integrability is needed at all.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  In Lemma 7.1's moment form the supremum over the label stays \emph{outside} the moment;
  inside it would cost $(\#\mathbb Z_L^n)^{1/2p}$, which at fixed $p$ no $N^{\varepsilon/2}$
  absorbs.  Stated through the row sums of \texttt{edgeKer}, so independent of (5.16).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The IBP input of (4.5), uniformly in time -- with its two closing lemmas retired]\label{lem:condstableflow}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_condRow_of_envelope, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_condRow_sub_self, RBM.Gauss.condRowReal_const, RBM.Gauss.inv_etaT_le_inv_etaT, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_greenDiagCentered_sub_minor, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_ibpRem_diag, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_ibpRem, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_condExpDiag_flow, RBM.Gauss.eq45Flow_of_unifDom_ibp, RBM.Gauss.eq45Flow_of_localLaw_gain, RBM.Gauss.eq45Flow_of_localLaw_gain', RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_flucRow_condExpDiag_psi, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_flucBlk_condExpDiag_psi}
  \leanok\uses{lem:condstable, lem:goodsetflow, lem:eq45inputs, lem:condrow}
  Run with the \emph{deterministic} control $\Psi^2$ instead of $L^{\max}$, the conditional
  expectation acts as the identity on the control, so the stability comparison degenerates and
  $\mathbb E_i[L^{\max}]\prec L^{\max}$ is never needed. $L^{\max}$ survives only through its lower
  half, which costs no power of $\eta$ -- so the fixed-time restriction of T119 is avoided rather
  than patched.

  \emph{\texttt{eq45Flow\_of\_localLaw\_gain} and its primed form are retired.}  Their gain slot
  asks for the unbudgeted interface at $B\asymp\Psi$, which \ref{thm:eq412-cond} shows cannot be
  filled; the unprimed one feeds into the same wall a fortiori.  Both now carry
  \texttt{@[deprecated]}, so that anyone using them again is warned by the compiler and not merely
  by a docstring -- the earlier experience with the all-levels good event is why that distinction
  was made.  The names stay in the list above because the declarations still exist; the live
  replacements are \texttt{eq45Flow\_of\_localLaw\_gain\_budget} and
  \texttt{eq45Flow\_of\_goodSetFlow\_budget} (\ref{thm:eq412-cond}), and end to end
  \texttt{eq45Flow\_of\_goodSetFlow\_highProb} (\ref{lem:eq412-small}).  Two consumers have
  \emph{not} been moved: \texttt{unifDomIcc\_flucRow\_condExpDiag\_psi} and its block twin still
  feed the retired form, still stand on the unbudgeted interface, and carry no warning of their
  own.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  At \texttt{UnifDomIcc} level no union over indices is taken, so the ported tool needs no
  cardinality hypothesis; the diagonal remainder is bounded by $\prec1$ rather than by a
  deterministic envelope that grows as $t_N\to1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The LDE bounds uniformly in time: a net engine with no lower bound on the control]\label{lem:ldeflow}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.stochDom_timeIcc_of_unifDom_relative, RBM.Gauss.close_of_holder_of_low, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_rowSum_general, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_sq_of_rowSum, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_ldeRow, RBM.Gauss.unifDomIcc_ldeCol, RBM.Gauss.LDENetClose, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_ldeRow_flow, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_ldeCol_flow, RBM.Gauss.ldeFlowDom_of_close}
  \leanok\uses{lem:eq45inputs, lem:goodsetflow, thm:lkdecayquant}
  The lower bound $N^{-B}\le\zeta$ that the earlier net engine assumed exists only to absorb the net
  error; replacing it and the modulus by the \emph{relative} comparison the absorption actually uses
  removes it entirely, and the old engine is a special case. The fixed-time inputs are then
  unconditional theorems, the vanishing fibres being handled per point.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  At \texttt{UnifDomIcc} level no union over the index set is taken, so the Gaussian row-sum moment
  bound feeds Markov with no cardinality hypothesis and no good event.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Lemma 5.7, estimate (5.35), in two shapes]\label{lem:5.7a}
  \lean{RBM.Lemma57.card_zdist_le, RBM.Lemma57.sqrt_tailT_le, RBM.Lemma57.le_sqrt_tailT, RBM.Lemma57.sqrt_rpow_neg_le_sqrt_tailT, RBM.Lemma57.exp_half_mul_exp_half_le, RBM.Lemma57.sum_sqrt_tailT_mul_le, RBM.Lemma57.card_zdist_le_real, RBM.Lemma57.sum_le_split_one, RBM.Lemma57.sum_le_split_two, RBM.Lemma57.loss32, RBM.Lemma57.one_le_loss32, RBM.Lemma57.case1_pointwise, RBM.Lemma57.case2_pointwise, RBM.Lemma57.sum_far_le, RBM.Lemma57.ellStarStar, RBM.Lemma57.inv_sq_le_tailT, RBM.Lemma57.eG_near_le, RBM.Lemma57.cNear, RBM.Lemma57.cFar, RBM.Lemma57.eG_far_le, RBM.Lemma57.eG_le, RBM.Lemma57.eG_le_paper, RBM.Lemma57.eG_le_reduced, RBM.Lemma57.eG_le_paper_of_schwarz, RBM.Lemma57.eG_le_reduced_of_schwarz, RBM.Lemma57.norm_mul_three_le, RBM.Lemma57.blkW, RBM.Lemma57.sum_block_ite_real, RBM.Lemma57.sum_blkW_normSq, RBM.Lemma57.sum_blkW_normSq_conj, RBM.Lemma57.norm_gloop_three_le_schwarz, RBM.Lemma57.gloop_rot1, RBM.Lemma57.gloop_h558a, RBM.Lemma57.gloop_h558b, RBM.Lemma57.gloop_h558a', RBM.Lemma57.gloop_h558b', RBM.Lemma57.gloop_two_re_nonneg, RBM.inv_sq_le_tailT, RBM.Lemma57.gloop_three_expand, RBM.Lemma57.sum_blockIndicator, RBM.Lemma57.sum_blockWeight, RBM.Lemma57.norm_gloop_three_le}
  \leanok\uses{lem:4.1, lem:stretched-exp, def:gloop}
  The drift estimate (5.35), pp.\ 59--61, proved rather than assumed: the near-field split
  (5.53)--(5.55), the far-field Case 1 (5.56)--(5.59) and Case 2 (5.60)--(5.63).  Two shapes are
  delivered -- the paper's, with $A^{-1/3}(J^*)^3$, and the one reduced by (2.73), whose far-field
  bracket is $r^{3/2}A^{-1/2}J^*+rA^{-1}(J^*)^{3/2}$: the first power of $J^*$ leading, $3/2$
  subleading.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  (5.62) needs the half-exponential kernel $\sum_b\sqrt{T(\|a_1-b\|)T(\|a_2-b\|)}\lesssim
  \ell_uA_u^{-1}\sqrt{T(\|a_1-a_2\|)}$, the discrete form of the paper's integral; the repository
  had only the fully exponential one.  Case 2's $(J^*)^{3/2}$ is irreducible, and the reason is
  visible in the term: the branch is $J\sqrt J\sqrt{T_{12}}\sum_b\sqrt{T_{1b}T_{2b}}$, closed by
  $\sqrt{T_{12}}\cdot\sqrt{T_{12}}=T_{12}$, so replacing any one of the three tail factors by
  (2.73) leaves a $T^{1/2}$ missing.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The drift $F$ at general loop length, as a definition, and its bound (5.77)]\label{thm:driftdef}
  \lean{RBM.DriftDef.driftF, RBM.DriftDef.drift_split_gen, RBM.DriftDef.F_eq_driftF, RBM.DriftDef.Fpath_eq_driftF_of_lt_one, RBM.DriftDef.eGterm_eq_eG, RBM.DriftDef.driftF_eq_eG_add, RBM.DriftDef.driftF_zero_eq_eGpm_add_quadGlue, RBM.DriftDef.sum_couplingLen_erase_two, RBM.DriftDef.fastDecay_driftF, RBM.DriftBound.dTrunc, RBM.DriftBound.couplingLen_dTrunc, RBM.DriftBound.exists_loopData, RBM.DriftBound.norm_lk_le, RBM.DriftBound.cDrift, RBM.DriftBound.cErr, RBM.DriftBound.xiSum, RBM.DriftBound.main_term_le, RBM.DriftBound.norm_driftF_le, RBM.DriftBound.norm_Hyp_F_le, RBM.DriftBound.stochDom_norm_driftF, RBM.DriftBound.stochDom_det_of_xiRhs, RBM.DriftBound.DriftInputs, RBM.DriftBound.hdom_of_driftInputs, RBM.DriftBound.hFmom_of_driftInputs}
  \leanok\uses{thm:egdef, thm:momentduhamel, thm:loopito2, lem:decaybridge, lem:5.10}
  $F=\tilde E+\sum_{\ell_K\ge3}[K\sim(L-K)]^{\ell_K}+E^{((L-K)\times(L-K))}$, proved from
  \texttt{Hyp.F\_unique} rather than posited: $\tilde E_0$ cancels exactly and no remainder
  survives.  Two bridges were missing and are supplied here -- (5.77) states its decay for
  \texttt{Decay.eG} while the drift carries \texttt{Gauss.eGterm}, and (5.15)'s
  $\sum_{\ell_K\ne2}$ is shown to be the paper's $\sum_{\ell_K>2}$, truncated at $\ell_K\le n$.

  Lines 1--3 of (5.77) are then \emph{theorems about this pinned $F$}, not hypotheses on an
  abstract one: \texttt{norm\_driftF\_le} pointwise, \texttt{norm\_Hyp\_F\_le} for
  \texttt{MomentDuhamel.Hyp.F} through \texttt{Fpath\_eq\_driftF\_of\_lt\_one},
  \texttt{stochDom\_norm\_driftF} in $\prec$ form with a deterministic control, and
  \texttt{hFmom\_of\_driftInputs} closing \texttt{hFmom} by one bare application -- so the chain
  instantiates no \texttt{SumZeroDyn.Hierarchy}, uses no \texttt{Lemma510} field, and leaves no
  \texttt{hdom} gap.  Every $\eta_u^{-1}$ in (5.77) comes from the single identity
  $W(\ell+1)/A\le(K_{\mathrm{rad}}+2)/\eta_u$.

  \emph{What is still hypothetical, and two recorded defects.}  \texttt{DriftInputs} is an
  assumption: it packages Lemma 5.9's decay and Step 3's counting, neither of which mentions the
  drift, and it is produced elsewhere.  (5.77)'s third line is short one factor
  $\Xi^{(L-K)}_{u,1}$ -- the paper drops it as $\lesssim1$ by (2.68), but it is neither a constant
  nor part of $\Xi^{\mathrm{rhs}}$, so Lean carries it explicitly (paper-deltas \#121).  And
  collapsing the three lines into the single constant \texttt{cDrift} loses one power of
  $A=W\ell_u\eta_u$ against (5.133): for that shape use the two-tensor form in
  \ref{thm:step6}, not this one.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $\partial_u$ of the loop at $z_u$, the split of \texttt{genLK}, $\partial_u K=\mathrm{primRhs}(K)$
  at every length, and (5.19) for the $\ell_K=2$ branch.  Both truncation bounds are theorems about
  \texttt{cutGlue}, not conventions.  For the size estimate, \texttt{dTrunc} is a workaround and
  not mathematics: \texttt{Decay.norm\_couplingLen\_le} quantifies its decay hypothesis over
  $|J|<m$ including $|J|=0$, where $L_\emptyset=LW$ and $K_\emptyset=0$ force $\Phi\ge LW$ and
  destroy the main term, although cut-and-glue never produces a loop of length below 2; zeroing
  the coupling below length 2 avoids it without touching \texttt{Decay.lean}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[$E^{(\tilde G)}$ as a definition, and the drift it splits]\label{thm:egdef}
  \lean{RBM.EGDef.eGpm, RBM.EGDef.eGpm_eq_eGterm, RBM.EGDef.gloop_three_conj, RBM.EGDef.gloop_k1_eq, RBM.EGDef.norm_gloop_k2_eq, RBM.EGDef.sum_norm_SB_col, RBM.EGDef.norm_eGpm_le, RBM.EGDef.eGpm_le_reduced, RBM.EGDef.fderiv2_comp_clm, RBM.EGDef.contDiffAt_gloop_matrix, RBM.EGDef.coordD2_gloop_eq, RBM.EGDef.coordD2_sub_const, RBM.EGDef.genLK_eq_split, RBM.EGDef.hasDerivAt_Kval_two, RBM.EGDef.primBilLen_two_of_len_two, RBM.EGDef.couplingLen_two_of_len_two, RBM.EGDef.genS_eq_thetaGenLoop, RBM.EGDef.drift_split, RBM.EGDef.F_eq_eGterm_add_quadGlue, RBM.EGDef.F_eq_eGpm_add_quadGlue, RBM.EGDef.primBil_two_eq}
  \leanok\uses{lem:5.7a, thm:momentduhamel, thm:loopito2, thm:generator}
  (5.51)'s $E^{(\tilde G)}$ written out in $G$-loops as a \emph{definition}, proved equal to
  (2.47)'s $\tilde E$; and the identity, at loop length two, between the drift $F$ that
  \texttt{Hyp.drift} pins down and $E^{(\tilde G)}$ plus (5.49)'s quadratic gluing term -- with no
  third term.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  At length two the only cut is $(k,l)=(1,2)$ and both sub-loops have length two, so the coupling
  \emph{is} its $\ell_K=2$ component and the sum over $\ell_K>2$ is empty; (5.19) identifies that
  component with the \texttt{genS} already on the left of \texttt{Hyp.drift}.  Hence every quantity
  on the left of (5.35) is either a definition or pinned by an identity, and no
  \texttt{SumZeroDyn.Hierarchy} is instantiated.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Lemma 5.7, estimate (5.36), free of the stopped martingale]\label{lem:5.7b}
  \lean{RBM.Lemma57.loss1, RBM.Lemma57.cNear2, RBM.Lemma57.inv_four_le_tailT_sq, RBM.Lemma57.ee_near_le, RBM.Lemma57.case2a_pointwise, RBM.Lemma57.case2b_pointwise, RBM.Lemma57.sum_ee_far_le, RBM.Lemma57.cFar2, RBM.Lemma57.ee_far_le, RBM.Lemma57.ee_le, RBM.Lemma57.ee_le_paper, RBM.Lemma57.ee_le_sym, RBM.Lemma57.ee_le_paper_sym, RBM.EEDef.ee_le_EEpath_sym, RBM.EEDef.ee_le_paper_EEpath_sym, RBM.EEDef.ee_sym_hyp_consistent}
  \leanok\uses{lem:5.7a, lem:4.1, lem:stretched-exp}
  The $E\otimes E$ bound (5.36), pp.\ 61--63, with the $T_{u,D}^2$ normalization and the spatial
  decay.  In the library (5.36) had existed only through the stopped-martingale hypothesis, which
  the moment route cannot use; it is now a pointwise estimate, applying to the pinned
  \texttt{EEpath} and free of the Cauchy--Schwarz and symmetry hypotheses: both are discharged by
  cutting (5.23)'s 6-loop at whichever of its two glue labels leaves the decaying edges, which is
  what (5.22)'s $k=1$ / $k=0$ symmetry amounts to.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  The super-linearity in $J^*$ here is intrinsic: Case 2(1a)'s four $G$ edges split into two pairs
  each yielding $J^*T$, and the left side needs $T^2$, so neither tail factor can be traded for
  (2.73); Case 2(1b)'s third tail factor is what the convolution needs to rebuild
  $T(\|a_1-a_2\|)$.  Their sizes differ, though: (1a) carries $A^{-1/2}$ -- the
  $(W\eta_u\ell_u)^{-1/2}$ that (5.36) itself displays -- and only (1b) carries $A^{-1}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[(5.36) applied to the pinned $E\otimes E$]\label{lem:5.7c}
  \lean{RBM.EEDef.glueSum, RBM.EEDef.glueSum_nonneg, RBM.EEDef.norm_eeTens_le_W_sum, RBM.EEDef.eeL6, RBM.EEDef.eeL6_eq_glueSum, RBM.EEDef.eeL6_nonneg, RBM.EEDef.norm_eeFun_le_W_sum, RBM.EEDef.norm_EEpath_le_W_sum, RBM.EEDef.norm_eeField_le_W_sum, RBM.EEDef.one_le_W, RBM.EEDef.ee_le_EEpath, RBM.EEDef.ee_le_paper_EEpath, RBM.EEDef.lab₁, RBM.EEDef.lab₂, RBM.EEDef.ee_le_EEpath_labels, RBM.EEDef.ee_hyp_consistent}
  \leanok\uses{lem:5.7b, lem:eebridge, thm:discharge-bdg}
  (5.36) holds for $\|(E\otimes E)_{u,\sigma,c}\|$ with $E\otimes E$ the \emph{definition}
  \texttt{MomentDuhamel.EEpath} $=$ \texttt{EEBridge.eeField}, not an abstract field.  The
  $b$-sum $L^{(1)}$ of (5.22) is likewise a definition, \texttt{EEDef.eeL6}, so the hypotheses
  \texttt{hEE}, \texttt{hL6} and $1\le W$ of the abstract form are discharged.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  (5.22) is already a theorem (\texttt{eeEdge\_eq\_sum\_SB} read through the gluing identity), so
  $\|E\otimes E\|\le W\sum_b L^{(1)}(b)$ is the triangle inequality plus one exchange of the $k$-
  and $b$-sums.  Six hypotheses survive -- (2.73) at $n=6$, the far-$b$ remainder, (4.2)/(5.31),
  the Cauchy--Schwarz step (5.65)/(5.66), the $G^\dagger E_bG$ bound (5.72), and the $k=2$ half
  \texttt{hsym} -- and they are shown jointly satisfiable by an explicit witness, so the
  conclusion is not vacuous.  \texttt{h566} needs the $6$-loop entrywise expansion and
  \texttt{hsym} needs the second term of (5.22); neither is in the repository.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The deterministic modulus of (4.2), and why the floor cannot be removed]\label{lem:ldenetclose}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.norm_mul_mul_apply_le_of_entries, RBM.Gauss.norm_green_sub_apply_le_of_entries, RBM.Gauss.norm_greenMinorMat_flow_sub_apply_le, RBM.Gauss.minorCol_eq_greenMinorMat, RBM.Gauss.minorRowConj_eq_greenMinorMat, RBM.Gauss.norm_minorCol_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_minorRowConj_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_minorCol_flow_sub_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_minorRowConj_flow_sub_le, RBM.Gauss.sWeight, RBM.Gauss.rowLin, RBM.Gauss.rowSum_eq_rowLin, RBM.Gauss.abs_norm_sq_sub_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_Hflow_apply_le_opNorm, RBM.Gauss.abs_sWeight_sub_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_rowLin_le, RBM.Gauss.norm_rowLin_sub_le, RBM.Gauss.sWeight_minorCol_eq, RBM.Gauss.sWeight_minorRowConj_eq, RBM.Gauss.abs_ldeRow_flow_sub_le, RBM.Gauss.abs_ldeCol_flow_sub_le, RBM.Gauss.eventually_mod_le_floor, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_ldeRow_flow_floor, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_ldeCol_flow_floor, RBM.Gauss.ldeFlowDom_floor, RBM.Gauss.ldeNetClose_of_lower_bound}
  \leanok\uses{lem:ldeflow, lem:opnorm, lem:4.1}
  Both sides of (4.2) have a deterministic H\"older-$\tfrac12$ modulus along the flow, so (4.2)
  holds uniformly in $u\in[s_N,t_N]$ and in the pair \emph{with an additive floor}
  $N^{-B}$, for every $B$ -- with no good event beyond $\|X\|\le N$, no off-diagonal decay and no
  local law.  The floor is not an artefact: given the modulus, \texttt{LDENetClose} is
  \emph{equivalent} to a polynomial lower bound $N^{-B}\le\zeta(u)$, and $\zeta$ is exponentially
  small in the band distance -- which is what (2.76)/(4.3) say.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  The modulus is proved entrywise, the repo having no operator-norm bound for a principal
  submatrix; that costs two factors of $|\mathrm{Idx}|$, absorbed into the exponent $10$.  What is
  missing for a floorless statement is not a Gaussian-process tool -- a polynomial net already
  beats the tail $e^{-N^{2\tau}}$ -- but an anti-concentration lower bound on the minor Green
  column, which is in neither the repository nor the paper.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[(4.2) and (4.3) uniformly in time with a floor, and \texttt{Step1.Hyp} needing neither]\label{lem:entrybound-time}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.EntryBoundFlow', RBM.Gauss.entry_bound_stochDom_floor_idx, RBM.diag_bound_stochDom_floor_idx, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_add_const, RBM.Gauss.eventually_two_rpow_neg_one_le_W_inv, RBM.Gauss.entryBoundFlow_floor, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_offdiag_flow', RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_llMax_sq_flow_of, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_llMax_sq_flow', RBM.Gauss.lemma41Flow', RBM.Gauss.rpow_neg_one_le_etaT_of_scale_ge, RBM.Gauss.flowDelta_le_rpow_neg, RBM.Gauss.step1Hyp_gauss_of_scale', RBM.Gauss.DiagBoundFlow', RBM.Gauss.stochDom_normSq_Hflow_diag_idx, RBM.Gauss.eventually_rpow_neg_one_le_W_inv, RBM.Gauss.diagBoundFlow_floor, RBM.Gauss.stochDom_indicator_diag_flow', RBM.Gauss.lemma41Flow_of_diagBoundFlow, RBM.Gauss.lemma41Flow'', RBM.Gauss.lemma41Flow_gauss, RBM.Gauss.step1Hyp_gauss_of_scale''}
  \leanok\uses{lem:ldenetclose, lem:4.1, lem:step1hyp, thm:step1lift, lem:ldeflow}
  (4.2) and (4.3) along the flow, uniformly in $u\in[s_N,t_N]$: \texttt{EntryBoundFlow'} and
  \texttt{DiagBoundFlow'} restate them word for word with one additive floor $N^{-B}$ added to the
  control, and \texttt{entryBoundFlow\_floor} / \texttt{diagBoundFlow\_floor} prove them with no
  large-deviation hypothesis left, only the regime conditions.  The floorless forms are not merely
  unproved -- they are exactly the shape \ref{lem:ldenetclose} identifies as unavailable.  The
  floor costs nothing downstream: composed with the unconditional $W^{-1}$ bound and
  $3W\le WL\le N$, one may take $B:=1$.

  \emph{The consequence is that (4.2) and (4.3) have disappeared from \texttt{Step1.Hyp}.}
  \texttt{step1Hyp\_gauss\_of\_scale'} and \texttt{step1Hyp\_gauss\_of\_scale''} have hypothesis
  lists word-identical to the original minus \texttt{hEntry} and then also minus \texttt{hDiag},
  with nothing substituted for either; the two regime inputs they would have needed follow for
  free from the $N^c\le W\ell_t\eta_t$ the step already carries.  That the four downstream
  statements really are unchanged is \emph{compiled} rather than eyeballed, by
  $\texttt{h}_{\text{unprimed}}=\texttt{h}_{\text{primed}}$ \texttt{:= rfl} probes, which at the
  level of \texttt{Prop} demand definitional equality.

  Two things worth recording.  The row and column side had no analogue of the diagonal's
  index-widening at all -- a sharper gap than the one the diagonal had -- so
  \texttt{entry\_bound\_stochDom\_floor\_idx} was supplied and now sits beside
  \texttt{diag\_bound\_stochDom\_floor\_idx}, the two being the two halves of (4.2)/(4.3); its
  deterministic kernel is taken unchanged from the quadratic-form bound, nothing being reproved.
  And one shape difference that is \emph{not} a new assumption and not a paper-delta: (4.3)'s
  deterministic kernel carries a stability factor in $\kappa$, so \texttt{diagBoundFlow\_floor}
  takes $0<\kappa\le1$ with $|E|\le2-\kappa$ rather than $|E|<2$ -- free at the
  \texttt{Step1.Hyp} level, which carries both already.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  The time-uniform diagonal input is free but not automatic:
  $\|H_{u,ii}\|^2=u\,\omega_c^2\le\omega_c^2=\|H_{1,ii}\|^2$ with a control independent of $u$,
  so the bad event at any time is contained in the bad event at $u=1$; transferring it needs the
  subset lemma applied to a union, since the index types differ.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The joint derivative in flow time and spectral time]\label{lem:loopjoint}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.bddC2C_loopObs_ball, RBM.Gauss.testFun_momentFun_loopObs_ball, RBM.Gauss.half_abs_im_le_abs_im_of_mem_ball, RBM.Gauss.green_mul_self_mul_green, RBM.Gauss.norm_fderiv_Gsig_flow_le, RBM.Gauss.BddC1On, RBM.Gauss.bddC1On_clm_apply, RBM.Gauss.bddC1On_matrix_mul, RBM.Gauss.bddC1On_gloopProdFlow, RBM.Gauss.bddC1On_gloop_flow, RBM.Gauss.hasFDerivAt_Gsig_flow, RBM.Gauss.clm_prod_eq_smulRight, RBM.Gauss.measurable_deriv_of_hasDerivAt_zero, RBM.Gauss.differentiableAt_integral_gloop_flow}
  \leanok\uses{lem:loopc2, thm:loopito, def:gauss-model}
  Uniformity in $z$ is free, but it is not what was needed: the joint derivative also moves the
  flow, and $\partial_vH_v=(2v)^{-1}H_v$ -- the direction \emph{is} the matrix, so
  $GMG=G+\zeta G^2$ lets the resolvent absorb it and the dominating function is a \emph{constant},
  with no moments of $\|X\|$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Measurability of $\omega\mapsto\mathrm{fderiv}$ is obtained with no formula for the derivative: a
  functional on $\mathbb R\times\mathbb R$ is determined by its two coordinate values, each a
  pointwise limit of difference quotients.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The two carried inputs of \texttt{EE\_le}]\label{lem:xilow}
  \lean{RBM.LKDecayQuant.GLoopDecayEvent, RBM.LKDecayQuant.gLoopDecayEvent_eq_lDecayEvent, RBM.LKDecayQuant.highProb_gLoopDecay_of_flowInputs, RBM.LKDecayQuant.highProb_eeDecay_of_flowInputs, RBM.LKDecayQuant.highProb_eeDecay_of_inputs, RBM.Gauss.sum_gloop_ofFn, RBM.Gauss.commute_green, RBM.Gauss.entryMax, RBM.Gauss.inv_le_loopMax, RBM.Gauss.rpow_neg_le_xiL, RBM.Gauss.highProb_xiLowEvent}
  \leanok\uses{lem:absorb, thm:lkdecayquant, thm:trace-moment, lem:eebridge}
  The decay input needed no new estimate: the sample's loop \emph{is} \texttt{gloop} of the flow, so
  the existing decay at every length gives the glued one as an instance. The lower bound on
  $\Xi^{(L)}$ is proved by \emph{summing} over labels rather than maximising:
  $\sum_aL_{\sigma,a}=W^{-m}\operatorname{Tr}\prod_iG(\sigma_i)$, and at the alternating charge
  $G$ commutes with $G^\dagger$, so the trace is $\sum_{xy}|(G^k)_{xy}|^2$ -- manifestly positive.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  Spectrum, positive-semidefinite order and Cauchy--Schwarz are all avoided; the quantitative part
  is entrywise bookkeeping against $\operatorname{Tr}G^k(H-z)^k=\mathrm{card}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The cut-and-glue algebra, and (5.19) with the missing $S^{(B)}$]\label{thm:loopito2}
  \lean{RBM.Gauss.coordD2_loopObs_eq, RBM.Gauss.isHermitian_Bmat_of, RBM.Gauss.sumSblk_half_wirtPair, RBM.Gauss.loopIto_second_frozen, RBM.Gauss.loopIto_second_frozen_of_im_ne_zero, RBM.Gauss.loopItoFrozen, RBM.Gauss.generator_add_zMotion_gauss, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_sample_ELval_hierarchy_gauss, RBM.Gauss.thetaGenOp, RBM.Gauss.thetaGenLoop, RBM.Gauss.thetaGenLoop_ofFn, RBM.Gauss.thetaGenLoop_kernel_eq, RBM.Gauss.smul_thetaGenOp_eq, RBM.Gauss.sum_Theta_mul_SB_row, RBM.Gauss.thetaGenOp_eq_ThetaOp, RBM.Gauss.thetaGenLoop_three, RBM.Gauss.couplingLen_two_eq_thetaGenLoop, RBM.Gauss.hasDerivAt_sub_prim_thetaGen}
  \leanok\uses{thm:loopito, lem:loopc2, lem:5.10, thm:moment-hierarchy}
  $\tfrac12\sum_{ij}S_{ij}\partial_{ij}\partial_{ji}L=\tilde E+\mathrm{primRhs}$ exactly, with no
  boundary term: the contraction $\tfrac12\sum_{ij}S_{ij}\langle ABCB\rangle
  =\tfrac12W\sum\langle AE_a\rangle S^{(B)}_{ab}\langle CE_b\rangle$ is where Definition 2.10's
  $E_a$ come from. (5.19) holds for the symmetrized coupling and the kernel
  $\xi\Theta_{t\xi}S^{(B)}$ -- (5.16) as printed omits the $S^{(B)}$.  That omission has since
  been corrected in \texttt{RBM.ThetaOp} itself, so \texttt{thetaGenOp} is now the same
  operator, by \texttt{rfl}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $\xi\Theta_{t\xi}S=t^{-1}(\Theta_{t\xi}-1)$; the omission is invisible to every row-sum argument
  because $S^{(B)}$ is row-stochastic, so only the identity itself fails.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Stretched-exponential calculus, (5.27)--(5.32), (5.49)--(5.50), (5.62), (5.72), (7.12)]\label{lem:stretched-exp}
  \lean{RBM.sqrt_add_le_add_sqrt, RBM.sqrt_sub_sqrt_le_sqrt, RBM.sqrt_add_le_of_le, RBM.sqrtExpPrim, RBM.hasDerivAt_sqrtExpPrim, RBM.continuous_sqrtExpPrim, RBM.integral_exp_neg_mul_sqrt, RBM.integral_exp_neg_mul_sqrt_le, RBM.integral_Ioi_exp_neg_mul_sqrt, RBM.integral_Ioi_exp_neg_sqrt, RBM.exp_mul_sqrt_triangle_le, RBM.integral_exp_mul_sqrt_triangle_le, RBM.integral_exp_sqrt_triangle_le, RBM.integral_exp_half_sqrt_triangle_le, RBM.sum_range_exp_neg_mul_sqrt_le, RBM.sum_exp_neg_mul_sqrt_zdist_le, RBM.sum_exp_neg_mul_sqrt_zdist_div_le, RBM.tailT, RBM.ratioJ, RBM.ellStar, RBM.rpow_neg_le_tailT, RBM.tailT_nonneg, RBM.tailT_pos, RBM.tailT_antitone, RBM.le_ratioJ_mul_tailT, RBM.one_le_ratioJ, RBM.sqrt_log_rpow_three_halves, RBM.tailT_sub_le, RBM.eventually_exp_mul_log_rpow_le, RBM.unifDetDom_tailT_sub, RBM.exp_neg_sqrt_mul_exp_neg_sqrt_le, RBM.zdist_sub_le_add, RBM.sum_tailT_mul_tailT_le, RBM.mul_sum_tailT_mul_tailT_le, RBM.mul_sum_tailT_mul_tailT_le', RBM.sqrt_zdist_sub_sqrt_zdist_le}
  \leanok
  $T_{u,D}(\ell)=(W\ell_u\eta_u)^{-2}e^{-(\ell/\ell_u)^{1/2}}+W^{-D}$ is non-increasing and
  $T(\ell-C\ell^*_u)\prec T(\ell)$; $\int_0^ae^{-\sqrt{a-x}-\sqrt x+\sqrt a}\,dx\le16$,
  $\int_0^\infty e^{-\sqrt x}=2$; the convolution bound
  $W\sum_xT(\|a_1-x\|)T(\|a_2-x\|)\le C\eta_u^{-1}(W\ell_u\eta_u)^{-1}T(\|a_1-a_2\|)$ (up to
  $W^{-D}$ terms); and (7.12).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $\sqrt{x+y}\le\sqrt x+\sqrt y$, closed-form antiderivatives of $e^{-k\sqrt x}$, and
  comparison of lattice sums with integrals.
\end{proof}

\chapter{Delocalization from the local law}

\begin{lemma}[Eigenvector bound (2.10), proof of Theorem 2.2]\label{eq:2.10}
  \lean{RBM.green, RBM.green_eq_spectral, RBM.im_green_apply_self,
    RBM.sq_norm_eigenvector_le_sum, RBM.sum_eq_mul_im_green,
    RBM.sq_norm_eigenvector_le_im_green, RBM.sq_norm_eigenvector_le_of_norm_green_le,
    RBM.sum_sq_norm_eigenvectorBasis, RBM.abs_poisson_sub_le, RBM.im_green_lipschitz_energy, RBM.sq_norm_eigenvector_le_of_norm_green_le_near, RBM.eventually_forall_measure_index_le, RBM.StochDom.of_forall_seq, RBM.netDen, RBM.netDen_pos, RBM.netDen_cast_pos, RBM.bulkNet, RBM.abs_bulkNet_le, RBM.exists_bulkNet_close, RBM.card_bulkNet_le, RBM.Band.rpow_le_zScale, RBM.netDen_inv_div_rpow_le, RBM.delocalization_of_Thm221N', RBM.delocalization_of_Thm221N}
  \leanok
  Let $H$ be Hermitian with eigenpairs $(\lambda_l,\psi_l)$ and $G(z)=(H-z)^{-1}$.
  For $\eta>0$,
  \[ |\psi_k(x)|^2 \le \sum_l \frac{\eta^2|\psi_l(x)|^2}{(\lambda_k-\lambda_l)^2+\eta^2}
     = \eta\,\operatorname{Im} G_{xx}(\lambda_k+i\eta). \]
  Hence $|G_{xx}(\lambda_k+i\eta)|\le C$ implies $|\psi_k(x)|^2\le C\eta$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}\leanok
  $G = U\operatorname{diag}((\lambda-z)^{-1})U^*$ gives
  $\operatorname{Im}G_{xx}(E+i\eta)=\sum_l \eta|\psi_l(x)|^2/((\lambda_l-E)^2+\eta^2)$;
  at $E=\lambda_k$ the $l=k$ term alone is $|\psi_k(x)|^2/\eta$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}\label{rem:2.2}
  Theorem 2.2 itself follows by feeding in the local law (Theorem 2.3) at
  $\eta=N^{-1+\tau}$, which gives $|G_{xx}|=O(1)$ with high probability.  That input
  is probabilistic and belongs to the stochastic layer; the deterministic step above
  is what is formalized.
\end{remark}

\chapter{The stochastic layer (out of scope for now)}

\begin{remark}\label{rem:stochastic}
  Sections 2.4, 2.6, 2.7 and 5--7 of the paper rest on It\^o calculus for matrix
  Brownian motion, which Mathlib does not have: there is no It\^o formula, no
  matrix-valued SDE theory, and no Dyson Brownian motion.  The loop hierarchy
  (2.45), Lemmas 2.18--2.20 and Theorem 2.21 are therefore left as stated
  hypotheses, so that the logical skeleton of the main theorems still typechecks.
\end{remark}
